Rūpiṇī, through illusion, is woman, And skillful means has the action of man; Nondual, woman and man are one. Not one, and not not one, is Rūpiṇī. In things such as the objects of form, Rūpiṇī is without signifier or signified. Hard to signify, she is the signified; in the signified, The signified is formless: she is the characteristic. That domain which cannot be signified Is the domain of that signifier. Just as someone sees in a dream, And even when seeing himself dead, Weeps while he is seeing it, So is Rūpiṇī of one's own form and of others'. Thus, as the nature of all: Vajrasattva's own nature, The nature of the great being, The great pledge being, The being in the manner of great awakening, The stable and the moving in all their variety. Such form, as before, Is not known by the logicians; It is not to be realized by the yoga of the childish. By attaining the supreme state of the yoginī, It comes to be known in this very life. All people have the nature of awakening, But deluded fools do not know it. All sentient beings are the reality of the yoginī; Whoever is a human being is Vajradhara. When this song was sung, all Were terrified and fell fainting to the ground. The yoginīs too had fallen down: Wondrous is the domain of the Dharma! The chief heroes, Skull Fragment and the rest, Roused them, saying: "Live! Live! You rākṣasīs, You who liberate the nature of the realm of beings! Between you and sentient beings there is no difference, You who liberate all the heroes!" Then once again, into the body Of the Blessed One, all of them entered. Then again, through the application of emanation, They were emanated from the mouth of the Lord. The causes, Vairocana and the rest, He again emanated from his mouth. The Blessed One, having gathered them all, Concerning the tantra of the yoginīs Made an inspired utterance, And concerning all the yoga tantras as well: Vairocana and Ratneśa, Here Passion, Amoghasiddhi, Akṣobhya, and the line of Locanā, Māmakī and Pāṇḍaravāsinī, Tārā and Rūpavajrā, Sound, smell, and taste likewise, Touch, and the one renowned as Dharmadhātu, Kṣitigarbha, Ākāśagarbha, Vajrapāṇi, Lokeśvara, Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, and Samantabhadra, Mañjuśrī and Maitreya, Yamāri and Prajñāntaka, Padmāntaka, Vighnāntaka, Acala and Nīladaṇḍa, Ṭakkirāja, Mahābala, Sumbha, Uṣṇīṣacakravartin, Bhṛkuṭī, and Parṇaśabarī; The rest is Vajrasattva. Thus these names of the tantra Are the characteristic of the reality of skillful means. The wisdom tantra is self-arisen; It abides always as its own nature. Among the factors of awakening It follows the indestructible state. At the time when the eon is burned, It goes elsewhere and truly abides. The heroes, Skull Fragment and the rest, Bear names such as Akṣobhya. By the enumeration of the divisions of entities, Many families arise. Relying on the abode of Rūpiṇī, The heroes come to stand in proud display. Form, the form that has become formless, Is carried along equally with Saṃvara. From the union of all the heroes, Vajrasattva, supreme bliss, Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata: The Blessed Lord spoke this.

This was the sixth chapter of the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, Great King of Yoginī Tantras, the rite on the nature of the characteristics of Rūpiṇī, the nature of the wheel of channels, the presentation of the abodes, and the characteristics of the tantras.

Next, the ground of the supreme yoga, Through the action of seeing the rite: That from which Kākāsyā arose, She who by her rite goes to liberation. She dwells in a subcontinent, With the transit of Taurus and the auspicious one; She should be known as the month of Vaiśākha, And is held to be the fifth ground. The goddess dwells at the gate. kā is perceived as the mother Dharmadhātu; The second is the Dharma body, And from it arises the enjoyment body; syā is the emanation body. From the navel, heart, throat, and head The four apertures should be known. She has a life-face like a crow. For her there is always the fierce rite: Living creatures are slain in an instant, Nāgas are to be summoned, Rain is brought down, and there is paralysis. The dharmadhātu and great awakening, She whose nature is all the yoginīs, In the Dharma, enjoyment, and emanation bodies Moves out of desire for bliss. Embodying the action of true mantra, Rely on her, the crow chief lady. In Vajravārāhī The crow lady reaches her abode. The thirty-seven yoginīs Arose from this mantra: oṃ ba na jra mo kā bha kā ga syā ba ya te ke bī hūṃ re hūṃ śā pha ya ṭa hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha svā ṭa hā pha oṃ ṭa svā hūṃ hā hūṃ. Through the distinction of long and short, This has the nature of wisdom and skillful means: The short is declared to be the nature of skillful means, And the long, wisdom. Black in color, the great fierce one; For the rest she is like Ḍākinī. Because the awakening mind melts, At that time she is the Victress among crows, Circling in the sky like a crow, Through the wind of Kākāsyā. Because she flies, and flies upward, Therefore she looks below and above. Having flown far like a crow, She comes back to the island of the crow. Arriving there is great bliss, Experienced with the body. Through meritorious karma one comes to be born, The yoga rising higher and higher.

Likewise here, one without merit
Goes to the hell of crows that tear one to pieces.
Among the modes of the perfections,
She is held to be the perfection of generosity.
Through taking what is not given, the hell-being
Is always hungry, like a crow.
Thus, at the time of her rite,
Whatever person dies a natural death,
Having meditated on that yoginī,
Comes to experience great bliss.
All the conduct of the yoginī as well
One comes to attain by oneself.
If no action is done, there is
No one possessed of karma and no agent of action.
The movement of one's own mind is karma,
And that karma is also the agent.
The shape of the body, embodying form,
Without mind, is the supreme being.
Whatever is done by all the hands and feet,
The agent of the action is oneself.
To treat all of that as of no account
Is the characteristic of right action.
In action, too, pursue virtue as the cause;
Nonvirtue should be abandoned by oneself.
The worship of the yoginīs,
And likewise the gatherings for feasting,
In villages, in towns, and in sacred sites,
And on the great peaks of mountains and the like,
Should be offered for the sake of the pledge.
The yoga grants the excellent fruit of accomplishment.
The reflections of Ḍākinī, too,
Bear the names of the pervading winds:
The life wind, the downward-clearing, the equal-abiding,
And the pervading, in a twofold order as is fitting.
Ḍākinī, likewise Lāmā,
Khaṇḍarohā and Rūpiṇī,
Ulūkāsyā, Śvānāsyā, and Śūkarāsyā;
In order, the nāga, tortoise, and lizard winds,
Devadatta and Dhanaṃjaya.
Yamadāḍhī and Yamadūtī, the two
That are called joy and sorrow,
Yamadaṃṣṭrī and the supreme Yamamathanī.
These words of brief teaching as well
I shall now explain at full length.
Vārāhī, having made offerings,
Asked Vajrasattva:
"As many as are the winds, the life wind and the rest,
The name perceived for each one of them:
O my Teacher, it is a great wonder!
They come to arise at all times."
The Blessed One declared:
"First, the one that holds the principal
Is liberated through the essence of emptiness.
The vajra-like wind is to be set in place;
Its support is the vessel world.
Because destruction comes to dissolve,
And it has no shape or form at all,
Therefore it is called downward-clearing,
And is taught as going and coming.
Having entered the family of the equal-abiding,
Whatever was formerly expressed by anyone
In speech comes to be spoken:
All the habitual tendencies of before.
The pervading, because it sets its seal
On all beings, is the wind of karma.
The nāga is the very essence of anger.
The pervading likewise has two terms.
The tortoise, whose essence is avarice,
Has the characteristic of speech.
The lizard is attachment and the equal-abiding.
The downward-clearing is jealousy and Devadatta.
Dhanaṃjaya embodies delusion.
Of the two counts of the life wind,
It should be known as possessing the wisdom of joy;
Sorrow is a designation of it.
Therefore the wise, discerning each one individually,
Should know: because the life wind arises as the self of all.
Just as, of the twelve grounds,
The first is itself the twelfth,
It should be known through the ten,
Because it is lord of the ten grounds.
Through the natures of a single ground
She is held to be the great Vajradharā.
Whatever perfections there are, generosity and the rest,
Are themselves coemergent perfect awakening.
Because she has entered within all,
She is the supreme Vajradhātvīśvarī.
Whatever is explained or not explained,
All of it has the mode of awakening.
Again, the goddess is in the shape of a crow;
Then she is Rūpiṇī of action.
The action is in the places of the directions,
The apertures of all the sites of the channels.
Dissolving, and rising from that, is meditative concentration;
It is perceived under the name Kākāsyā.
Thus all the treasures of yoga
Are clearly obtained from the mouth of the guru.
Whoever is the guru, do not despise him,
And hold that very teacher dear.
If one does not attain the state of the noble ones,
One is boiled in Avīci and the other hells.
Through devotion to the guru in every way
One will fully attain awakening."
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata:
The Blessed Lord spoke this.

This was the seventh chapter of the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, Great King of Yoginī Tantras, the rite on the characteristics of generating the life force of Kākāsyā and the others.

Next, the supreme characteristics of the others, Ulūkāsyā and the rest, which are clear, The action, the true deed, And all the junctures I shall explain. Gemini, Scorpio, and Jyeṣṭhā Abide always through union with the moon. Ulūkāsyā, having attained wisdom, Moves along the three paths of the channels. u: the fierce, majestic one who bares her fangs, From eating the power of the path of wisdom. lū: delighting in desire, of great magical illusion, Toward the births of the asuras and the rest, At the twelve at the tip of the big toe of the foot. ka: she comes to move with the force of an arrow. In the name of Ulūkāsyā, too, u is held to be the aperture of Oḍiyāna. lā: it is renowned as Pullīramalaya. ū is the field of Mummuni. kā is Kāmarūpa. Likewise sya here is held to be all the fields. All the fields having become breath Is the culmination of bliss in the form of a triangle, Because it is the inexhaustible state of Heruka, Embodying the five sites. Or else, in my seat and the rest, Glorious Heruka is the vajra itself. The birthplace embodies the five sites: Heart, navel, palate, and head, And the secret place as well; it embodies The five apertures, the birthplaces of all the channels. The egg-born and the warmth-and-moisture-born, The womb-born and the miraculously born: These beings are the self of the qualities of the buddhas, Fully displaying the five families. She who dwells in a subcontinent, too, Truly abides in the form of the chief lady. Those born from the garland of mantra Are born embodying the thirty-seven: oṃ ba ma jra hā u ka lū lpa kā a syā gni ya saṃ ke ni hūṃ bhā hūṃ ya pha hūṃ ṭa hūṃ pha pha ṭa ṭa svā pha hā ṭa oṃ svā hūṃ hā. Thus the self of the reality of the mantra Has the nature of wisdom and skillful means. Greenish yellow in color, the great fierce one; For the rest she is like Lāmā. Having meditated on her in the center of the maṇḍala, Nondual with the site of the charnel ground, She is the domain of the expansive joy Of all the channels, the all-enjoying. This is the concentration of livelihood And the nature of reality in the form of luminosity. The rite of expulsion and the yoginī, The guide of the world: The various rites taught In the Dharma she herself taught are also to be done. Having perfected the yoga of all, This is the domain of this tantra.

Yet all these yogas as well,
In brief, are these words:
Withdrawal, and likewise
Meditative concentration, breath control, retention,
Recollection, and absorption:
The six characteristics of yoga.
Withdrawal is Kākāsyā;
Ulūkāsyā is held to be concentration;
Śvānāsyā abides as breath control;
For retention it is Śūkarāsyā;
Yamadūtī abides as recollection;
Absorption is held to be Yamadāḍhī;
Yamadaṃṣṭrī attains the extensive teaching;
Yamamathanī is the fruit, born as the self of all.
One who is liberated from saṃsāra
Has a body equal to an illusion.
This self-blessing, the subtle yoga,
And the very characteristic of vajra recitation,
The treasure of the mind of perfect awakening:
Beyond these, what remains is union.
This, too, is what is called nirvāṇa:
By all the paths one passes into nirvāṇa.
Fully entering the whole of the Great Vehicle,
It is practiced in the Vehicle of Mantra.
Deeds, treatises, and the various virtues
Are done with compassion, with pleasure and kindness.
Toward whatever entities and things there are,
Through the self-arisen in the form of wisdom,
From a mental continuum of that kind,
Yogins see their own selves.
The true characteristics of the body,
The tent of the channels, I shall explain.
From the crown of the head to the tip of the jewel,
The junctures and the throat are held to be twofold.
The big toes, the edge of the junctures,
Between the brows, and likewise the plume;
The root of the sexual organ, the forehead,
The eyes, and the two toes;
With the ring fingers, the knees should be known.
The calves and the two ears,
The soles of the feet and the heart,
The navel and the spine,
The anus and the palate should be known.
The ankles and the lips,
The cheeks and the hollows of the cheeks,
The groin and the two armpits,
The wrists and the feet,
The shoulder joints and the two breasts,
The nape and the shoulder blades: here these are the junctures.
The tailbone and the two jewels,
The joints of the waist and the throat,
The forehead and the two joints,
The ear holes and the root,
Are held to be the junctures of the two sides of the chest.
The buttocks and the hollows of the brows,
The tongue and the nose of a person,
And likewise the palate and the tongue,
The bolla and the kakkola, the two:
These and many others
Become the vital points of the channels and joints.
Each should be pressed in its own practice,
In the rite of applying the forceful method
Of the great-souled bodhisattvas;
This is to be known through the words of the guru.
On whatever lunar day of each month,
On that date there is the root of the channels,
From the first day of the bright half
Until the end of the dark half:
Know the channels where awakening flows down.
The Owl Lady acts at the gate.
Whatever her face resembles,
Such is the mudrā of the fingers,
And the breath should be made like that.
It is held to embody outer and inner.
Or else all the channels
Are to be pressed, and all the joints,
One hundred and twelve in all,
In order, according to the faces.
There and there the vowels blaze,
The fierce illusion, at all times.
Therefore the gate called ā
Is examined by conception in words.
For the consolation of the tathāgatas,
It is to be conceived as Vajradhara.
Whatever it is by which one is bound,
The channel becomes great delusion.
By that it becomes its very nature,
Like a jewel of various forms.
In both the deluded and the wise
The lord, great liberation, abides.
People take various ones to be the maker,
The Lord of all beings;
Therefore those whose karma ever declines,
Through evil deeds done before,
Come to ruin. But in this teaching
One will attain buddhahood, the goal to be reached.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata:
The Blessed Lord spoke this.

This was the eighth chapter of the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, Great King of Yoginī Tantras, on the definitive mode of Ulūkāsyā, her generation through the true rite, and the characteristics of the life wind and the rest.

Next, I shall explain another matter of great meaning,
The divisions of the causes of Śvānāsyā,
By merely understanding which
Yogins will accomplish the supreme.
śvā: the dog, the yoginī, breathes out;
By herself she comes to enter and come forth.
At all times, in sleeping
And in waking, bliss is a great quality.
na: having gone into the various places,
One comes to touch bliss by oneself.
The hundred and twenty
Are the characteristic of what the etymology teaches.
Thus, in the name of the dog,
She should be known as the bestower of bliss.
In those birthplaces of the lotus,
āsya: the mouth of the lotus fully opened.
A generous nature, enjoyments, great dance,
Song and music, and sleep,
Touch, garments, and ornaments,
Flowers and other fragrant things are desired.
Harmoniously, the mothers of the navel and the rest
Have the speech of beautiful women;
In all things, the worship of the buddhas and the rest,
In drinking the drink of liquor,
In pleasure groves and on the banks of ponds,
In going to places of rest and the like,
In satisfaction and in feasts as one wishes,
The words of the guru are great bliss.
The flirting of men and women,
And whatever acts of laughter and flirtation there are,
The holy mind of the wise
Becomes the very lordship of people.
Through worship and prostration and the like
To the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha,
Bliss comes from devotion to the guru,
And from offering the fee, and from women.
In deportment and the other dharmas
One does not engage in every action.
Defeating opponents,
Establishing the teachings of the buddhas,
The yoginīs, food and drink,
Meditation, and the generation of joy,
Born as the embodiment of the two organs;
Happiness is to be sought through the acts
Of the perfections, generosity and the rest;
Loving-kindness, compassion, joy,
And last of all, equanimity;
Speaking of wisdom and of consciousness,
And establishing the way of the Middle;
Giving consolation to all,
Refuting deprecation,
The acts of the ten virtues,
And the supreme acts of reading and recitation;
In order to behold the nondual hero
Who abides on the path of awakening,
Without regard for the body,
Dwelling in village after village:
Thus is the first stage.
The second I shall explain in turn.
Delighting in the tantras of the buddhas,
Being clear about the ultimate truths,
In the abodes of the yoginīs
Speaking the talk of accomplishment,
Perceiving the Dharma as of great meaning,
The meditator who perceives the right time:
The perception of abandoning the six destinies
Bears the name of the realization of reality.

Perceiving things as illusions, Bliss through the rites of pacifying and the rest, Not being one with father and son, Delighting in going to the sacred sites and the rest, Wholly giving up the heap of afflictions, Generating the heap of wisdom, Never coming back again to saṃsāra, Endowed with the yoga of Cakrasaṃvara; Holding to the words of the discerning, One should reject the words of those without realization. Not at odds with the senses, Beautiful by its very nature, The innate, and the enjoyment body, The emanation body, and the dharmakāya too, In waking perception and the rest: Since in the ocean of existence all is to be abandoned. The remaining four joys Are aspiration, the Kṛta age and the rest. In the Kṛta age the natural arises; The joy that is buddha is the Kṛta age. For beings in the Tretā age the subtle arises; In that awareness the coarse arises. In the Dvāpara age there is no bliss; The Kali age is coarse in part. All attain the qualities of a buddha; Through aspiration comes one's own fruit. Thus, through the phases of bliss, There are thirty-two great marvels. In the bliss of the third phase, By constantly examining the sixteen joys, They are to be known as sixteen elements. The arising of the natural becomes clear. It should be known to be like medicine: By eating the nectar one goes to the far shore. Padminī, Śaṅkhinī and Citriṇī, Hastinī and the one named for the bee, Cakrā, Vajrā, the pale-nosed one, Dhūmā, and the one at the birth of a child, The one adorned with ointment, Lajjā, Lightning, Candana, Sun-born, Red, and likewise Snake-eye: These should be known as the supreme medicine. At the time of joy through bliss, From the instant of sewing with a needle and the like, One remembers those tasks only in passing; The instant of meditation goes to its end. What is taught for the four phases Is a count of eight for those with minds: Going and transferring, likewise Vomiting and drowsing, likewise Falling and dripping, and here Trembling and toppling over. The eight yoginīs, Gaurī and the rest, Are the phases through the cause of bliss. Thus in the four kinds of phase Perception comes about through the name Śvānāsyā. In this state of right mindfulness, In what and in whom does she abide? Abiding in right effort, Śvānāsyā is in another's village. She is the very nature of the union of true mantra, Meditated upon in the center of the maṇḍala. oṃ ba ja jra ṭā śvā ma na ta ā ku syā ṭa ya u ke ta hūṃ ka hūṃ ṭā pha ya ṭa hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha svā ṭa hā pha oṃ ṭa svā hā This mantra made of thirty-seven Is wisdom and means, and grants accomplishment. Red in color, the great fierce one, In the rest she is like Khaṇḍarohā. In Āṣāḍha, in Karkaṭa, a widow, She who abides firmly with her own lord, She who dwells in the subsidiary island: Beings are born each according to their own nature. She is the goddess for the rites of subjugation; Gods, asuras and the rest, the knowers of mantra, The five nectars, and the great Tārā, Are for all the acts of offering. Having drawn it fully into the lotus at the base of the heart, The nectar born from all the words, Through the skilled use of the vajra tube, Through kindling, heating and the like, And by the mantras of body vajra and the rest, It is applied to what has been consecrated. From the union of all the heroes, Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss, Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata, The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the ninth chapter, the rite of the characteristics of Śvānāsyā and of the phases of bliss and the rest, from the Great King of Tantras of the Yoginīs, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas.

Next, of Śūkarāsyā I shall explain, in brief and at length: Fully entering into all things, And turning back from those things. Vārāhī, in the rites of paralysis, Is the crusher of the mountain of ignorance. sū The needle is the vajra point, and she Goes everywhere like a thread: The great joy of the mind of awakening. ka The skeleton brings forth the nectar. Its nature is desire and all the rest; She is beyond the fourth and unchanging. Then the eight great goddesses Are the characteristics of producing the womb and the rest. First the seed, like the crow, Falls into the mother's lotus. After one night it divides, And later is like dwelling as the owl. Having remained for one month, From the increase of Śvānāsyā's breath, As the sow too it comes to have limbs. When Yamadāḍhī tears open the womb, Yamadūtī is the child, and later death; Yamadaṃṣṭrī, by destroying karma, And Yamamathanī delight in the limbs of awakening, She from whom the fruit of all accomplishments arises. This right effort: Through the views of others, the fish and the rest, All engagements are true; Non-engagement is the sphere of that reality. Of bipeds, quadrupeds and the rest She is the crusher of the limbs of effort. Even through seeing and touching, That very non-engagement is constant. Yellow in color, the great fierce one, In the rest she is like Rūpiṇī. In the center of the vajra maṇḍala, Having the nature of wisdom and means, In Siṃha, in Śrāvaṇa, in Makara, She dwells in the subsidiary island. The goddesses arisen from mantra Belong each to her own field and near field. oṃ ba daṃ jra ṣṭra sū ka ka rā rā lo syā gra yai mu hūṃ kha hūṃ bhi pha ṣa ṭa ṇā pha ya ṭa hūṃ svā hūṃ hā pha oṃ ṭa phaṭ svā hā The yoginī, the supreme chief lady Made of thirty-seven: The laying of the maṇḍala lines and the rest Belong to the mother of the applied meditation. Having laid the two great lines, The eastern and the respective desired lines, And having laid the two diagonal lines, One should drive in the pegs. From the sides of the brahmā line Lay off two thirty-second parts. The door is an eighth of the wheel, And the central lotus is equal to it. From the edge of the petals of the heart, That too should be known in three parts. One part of it is the three wheels; The remainder is the colored ground. A third of that is the garlands: The vajra, the lotus and the wheel. The spokes are less than the central wheel, And made thick by binding two into one. The pillars are set all around, Eight times three in the three wheels. The portals are three times the door; The door frame measures one door unit. The platforms are half of that, And the edges and sides likewise. And here, the dhāraṇī band And the jewel garland band are half. The garland of heads is half of that, And the band of darkness likewise. From the tops of the pillars tips protrude: Crossed vajras of great light. Small bells, nets and cloths, Banners and bells resound. Outside that, on all the lotuses, Draw correctly the heart syllables and the rest. And here, a garland of crossed vajras, And beyond it a garland of flames.

Outside that, the surrounding enclosure
Should be drawn in the order of the grounds.
A garland of curved knives should be set there,
And likewise heads and hands.
Outside the lotus of the heart
Draw a jewel garland correctly.
The eight charnel grounds should be drawn,
All around, Caṇḍogra and the rest.
The seed syllables should be arranged;
All of them arise from the curved knife.
Or else, with the letters
Of body and the rest, the whole maṇḍala;
Likewise, in the manner of an instant,
Bring forth the maṇḍala from the seed syllables.
On the petals of the lotus at the intermediate directions,
Skull cups of the five nectars
Should be placed upon vases;
Thirty-seven others are to be made.
Free of blackness and of flaws at the base,
With drooping spout and lofty neck,
With a pair of cloths about the necks,
They should be filled with the five jewels, grains and medicines,
Scented water and the tips of leaves,
And the five nectars.
The torma is for all the rites,
And all around, meditation and the rest.
To the vajra goddesses,
To the field protectors and the wrathful ones,
Offer the substances, the five nectars and the rest.
By satisfying them with the outer offerings and the rest,
One will thrive fully, without obstructors.
Afterwards, properly undertake the rites.
The goddess with many animal faces,
The thirty-seven embodied ones,
Having done the crushing with the action vajra,
Are endowed with the yoga of the three samādhis.
She is the fierce one with seventy-two arms,
Endowed with eighteen legs.
Her root face is a sow's face;
On the two sides there are nine and nine;
Above them, seven and seven;
And after that, three should be placed.
On the right: dog and jackal,
Cat, tiger and lion,
The terrifying one, the fanged one, horse and donkey:
Thus, in their order.
On the left: ox, buffalo, wolf,
Tortoise, makara and hare,
Rhinoceros, yak and muñja:
They should be depicted in their order.
Snake, weasel and lizard,
Otter, mouse and bear,
Garuḍa, vulture and crow,
Owl, heron and hawk,
Bat and ruddy goose,
Peacock, the copper-crested cock,
And pigeon: for all of these,
Their natural colors are to be intended.
What the maṇḍala is certainly to do
Is in the element of whatever nature it may be.
The self of thirty-three qualities
Is held by its own nature.
The faces that are its images
Are the very nature of bringing awakening to beings.
With her feet she tramples the hosts of bhūtas
Who have entered into the midst of the fire.
With the right: Viṣṇu and Rudra,
Brahmā, Sūrya and Agni,
Indra, Vāyu and Nairṛti,
And Kubera, in their order.
With the left: Yama and Kāma,
Candra, Varuṇa and Vāsuki,
Pṛthivī and Gaṇapati,
Kārttikeya and the wheel-turning king.
Thus these eighteen forms
Gathered together are praised as marvelous.
Through one's own mind, which comes to hold them,
The goddess's feet are vajra holders.
In the right hands, the array of weapons:
Vajra, sword and bladed spear,
Axe, curved knife and arrow,
Impaling stake, shield and hammer,
Wheel, ḍamaru and short knife,
Club and single-pointed lance,
Conch, copper trumpet and staff,
And likewise a peacock's tail,
Crow's feather and brush,
Fire hearth and mountain,
Club, mirror and lute,
Foot, head, hand and lung,
Entrails, Rāhu and iron chain,
Fetter, shackle and fishing net,
Severed head, blaze and black oil,
And terrifying forms, in their order.
In the left: bell, long shield and fang,
Hammer, noose and skull cup,
Bow, khaṭvāṅga and book,
Small handled shield and threatening finger,
Garland of jingling bells and iron chain,
Rock and charnel-ground dust,
Bhokaṃḍaka and fresh hide,
A dangling rope of hair,
Goad and corpse stick,
Rock, shield and head,
Skeleton and likewise sickle,
Eye, kidney and marrow,
Tamarisk stick and ritual dagger,
A handful of seeds and a saw,
Needle and the skin of a body,
A cloud pouring rain, and a wooden sword.
When they are known thus, in order,
They are the seventy-two channels of the tantra.
With the two additional arms
She wears an elephant hide above.
Seated upon the elephant of the three realms,
Tearing, she destroys the obstructors.
The color of all is their natural one,
Or else like the color of the principal lady.
The garland of heads and all the rest,
The clothes and ornaments, arise from the body.
Thus she is to be known
As the image of right effort.
By the nature of right mindfulness,
The distinction of things is the nature of bipeds.
Having filled everything with the net of samādhi,
Dissolve everything into luminosity!
Having realized this, one will attain buddhahood itself.
Various animals and the rest are born,
And in their own languages the Dharma
Will be taught to those born of their own kind.
Sealing all beings with the syllable sū,
One should recall that very innate nature.
The suffering of saṃsāra will not come again;
Those people who are like cattle become buddhas.
Through the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the tenth chapter, on generating Śūkarāsyā, entering the maṇḍala and the rest, from the Great King of Tantras of the Yoginīs, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas.

Next, here, the practice of Yamadāḍhī
I shall explain: meditators, listen!
In all, among the four seasons,
There are four of three months each.
The junctures bound by the solstices and the equinoxes
I shall explain fully.
The northern course and the rest:
Yamadāḍhī and the others dwell at the junctures,
She dwells in the subsidiary island.
The month of Mārgaśīrṣa and Dhanus,
And mohita, are always hers;
She is to be known in two rites.
She who with her fangs cuts off
The untimely death of meditators
Is therefore to be known as Yama's fang.
At first she has two syllables.
"Blessed One, how does untimely death
Come about for human beings?
By whose kindness shall I come to know it?
Who will appear for me as an emanation?"
The Blessed One declared:
"Yamadāḍhī, untimely death
Comes from beings' own individual karma."

Whatever evil of harming all people
One has done oneself,
Through that one dies an untimely death:
The elements and the breath are cut off.
Through being cut off by others,
Food and drink turn harmful.
If one gives gifts together with what one has gained,
One will not be ruined by an untimely death.
Giving is explained as twofold:
From the body and from the intention.
At the door of the left nostril,
Through the course of the moon's movement,
The moving life-breath is that of the sun;
Through the sequence of the wind of the avadhūtī,
By that the letter ya is explained.
The letter ma is she who flows in the center.
When the letter dā has gone to the right,
For a while the central one moves.
Through the letter a one attains abiding;
She who deceives is the one who goes in the sky.
The channels, which are the nature of all bliss,
Are known by the name of the letter ḍhi.
The letters in brief
Are said to be in the five maṇḍalas.
Moving them from their actual places,
You should know how to ransom untimely death.
Thus the single merit
Has a body that travels from place to place.
What is called death is conceptual thought;
This will lead to the state of a sky-goer.
Here the color becomes twofold,
And so too the meditation on the body.
According to the order of the ḍākinīs,
The signs, the weapons and the rest come about.
She is said to be known as the dharma of abandonment,
The principal lady at the center of the maṇḍala.
The yoga made of thirty-seven,
By the nature that arises from mantra:
oṃ āḥ s hūṃ ha ba sra jra bhu ya ja ma bhā dā su ḍhi rā kā ya yai hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ svā svā hā hā oṃ
This is the nature of wisdom and means,
The chief of yoginīs and heroes.
Through the application of Yama's fang,
One should devour Antaka, the ender of what is born.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the eleventh chapter, called the presentation of Yamadāḍhī and the characteristics of her generation, from the Great King of Tantras of the Yoginīs, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas.

Next I shall explain Yamadūtī, Through whom death is brought under control. She arose truly in Vṛścika, Together with Kārttika and Bhadrā. At the time of the spring equinox Is the characteristic of generating Yamadūtī. For her, the slayer of Yama, On the wheel for ransoming death, On a great wheel of twelve spokes, It should be turned around three times. The fives into twenty-five, Up to thirteen, should be drawn. Again, in the thirty-twos, Three, two and one are years. The hours, fire, two, the moon, The months, the lunar days, the directions and likewise the arrows; In the case of three, two and one, In order, and afterwards half a day and so on, One day, a day and two, Three and four, earth, and the directions of the sun, Wealth, and the directions of the waters, in order: It is clear as the nature of thirty-seven. What is to be known as the power of life Is nineteen in nature. Here again the nineteen Have the power whose nature is destroying. When the seven have arrived together at the sun, And the birth star and the moon Constantly move together as one, From that one truly attains the time of increase. The intervening days themselves Should be known to number sixteen, And through the sequence of seventy-two Apply them correctly in the sequence of years. Therefore the goddess Yamadūtī too Should be meditated upon as having the nature of the maṇḍala. Where the channel has given up the life-breath, There she should be made to descend. Above the branches, above the root, Breathing is done through the secret place. That is the equal state, all the junctures; She draws in the equal state with the letter ya. Then what is impaired by binding the vital points Is all explained through the letter ma. Having moved it below and above, Make the sharp fire blaze fiercely. Illnesses and many evils Will themselves be destroyed by this act. Then gifts should be given: Cattle, buffaloes, mounts and the rest. On the path of the mind of awakening, At that time drink that of the beautiful women. Then too, nasal medicine Arising from one's own limbs should be administered. One should eat together with milk Lungs and the rest, uncooked. If one obtains the great blood Of one who has himself fallen in battle, Abandoning doubt, one should drink it, And one should eat its flesh as well. Or else, in whatever place one is, Until the signs of death are exhausted and gone, It is well if one takes one's food with milk, Or else all kinds of meat. Covering oneself completely with soft clothes, At all times One should keep one's dwelling warm, And definitely stay close beside a fire. There one should also drink warm water, And eat small and light meals. One should meditate on Yamadūtī, Oneself having the nature of thirty-seven, Having the nature of the union of secret mantra, With the two colors of yellow and red. With the yellow she paralyzes death; With the red she draws it under control. Through the actual innate perfect awakening, Those that go below the navel, to the throat and to the palate, Through profundity, the life-breath that goes elsewhere Gathers into one another's cavities. Therefore meditate on the khaṭvāṅga, Through joining the limbs to the khaṭvāṅga. In the center of the heart lotus, by the mind, The maṇḍala has the proportions given before. The goddess's color is blue; Her shape and the rest are like Rūpiṇī's. oṃ ba pa jra ra ya śu ma pā dū śva tī dya ye ta hūṃ śū hūṃ la phaṭ kha phaṭ ṭvāṃ sva ga hā dhā ri ṇe hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā oṃ hūṃ Born as the nature of wisdom and means, Having the nature of the Great Illusion, All these yoginīs Should be meditated upon in the form of nectar. They are white at the heart; All the wheels should be meditated upon. Within them, their own names too, White in color and radiant, Should be meditated upon Within each of the openings of the channels. By drinking with the tip of the vajra Fresh and boiled goat's milk, The five nectars and the self-arisen, Even timely death is overcome. Since one attains that undefiled state itself, What talk is there of untimely death for one? Therefore the dharmatā of right abandonment Is to be known as Yamadūtī. The drinking with the vajra that has been explained Is held to be twofold by its action: One is explained as outer, And the second is held to be inner in nature. One should meditate on a three-pronged vajra; She moves through the six channels at their times. The life-breath held in the movement of the haṃsa Drinks the lower water; Whatever little of the five nectars and the rest Enters fully into its center: By drinking it, old age and death flee. This is the instruction of Vajradhara.

You abide as the yoginī of Yamadūtī;
Through her one attains once more freedom from old age and death.
The unsurpassed yoginī is she who drives death away;
The yoginī is possessed of neither evil nor merit.
That is equality: from the equal life-breath,
From the unmoving state of the effort
Of the left, the right and the center,
Again the letters ya and ma are intended.
Yamadūtī is the state in which the haṃsa of the two sides
Is exhausted in the center.
Thus her many aspects
Should be indicated by one of steady intellect.
What need is there here for much explanation?
The perfection of generosity and the rest,
Virtuous deeds too, should be practiced;
One body has many bodies.
Offerings should be made to the yoginīs,
And honor should be shown to the heroes.
Whatever anyone rightly asks of anyone,
That one should therefore surely give them.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the twelfth chapter, a concise instruction on the characteristics of generating Yamadūtī and the rest, and on meditating on the wheel for cheating death, from the Great King of Tantras of the Yoginīs, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas.

Next, Yamadaṃṣṭrī I shall explain: now listen! Through the faults of the habitual tendencies Of beings, the tent of the body is pierced and burned. According to the deeds anyone has done toward anyone, Piercing is held to come at its proper time. The eighty-four vital points, And the places wherever they lie: These are to be set in the center, And the good and the poor are to be indicated. Since the nonvirtuous is feared as death, It is indicated by pain at the vital points. If at a single time Shooting pain arises in three places in the palate, Then know that one will die in three days; There is no doubt about this. If there is shooting pain in the instep, And if there is shooting pain in the navel, O goddess, people Will die in two days. If in the insteps and soles of both feet, And in the eyes, there is shooting pain, Then within three months One goes into the power of the Death-maker. If there is shooting pain in the soles of the feet, And if there is shooting pain in the nose, One will die in three days; About this too there is no doubt. If at the time of defecating and urinating A sneeze occurs, Then by that measure of time One will surely die within a year. In the morning, five days; At midday, likewise ten; At the other noon, a half month; And at midnight, a month. If at these times of this life A sneeze occurs as before, One will surely die at that time; There is no doubt about this. If there is shooting pain in the māra hollow Of the nape of the neck, in five years One will die: Know that there is no doubt about this. O goddess, if one does not see the tip of the tongue, One will therefore die in three days. If there is shooting pain at the tip of the ear, Death comes in four months. If one does not see the pupil Of the left eye in a mirror, Then one will die in seven days; So it was taught in former births. If on the first day of the bright fortnight The semen becomes other than white, He will die in six months; If red, it will surely be within that time. If at the end of the full union Of bhaga and liṅga A sneeze occurs, in five months He will then fall. If at the time of union the sound of a bell Arises in the ears, death is clear: If Śakra has become balanced, It is in five months, without doubt. Likewise, if at a single time The moon and the sun Cause shooting pain to any being, He has one month to live. If in the four joints of the hands and feet And in the little finger Shooting pain comes at a single time, He will surely die within a month. When shooting pain comes as three stabs, If its power and strength equal one's own, Heroic from the big toes, People in the three worlds Are in the manner of a king of knots: This is always explained by that example. Then death comes in six months, If Rudra too has become balanced. The center of the heart, and likewise as before, And here too the domain of the throat: Death comes in two and a half months; This domain allows no deliberation. Again, if there is shooting pain at the crown of the head, The ocean of the three cities is burned: Know that death is immediate. So it is explained in the tent of the body. Likewise, shooting pain at the root of the ear Is surely explained as immediate death. Likewise, shooting pain between the eyebrows: One will die in that very instant. If the breath at the tip of the nose Becomes cold, it is the time of completion. All such deaths Are cheated through the state of Yamadaṃṣṭrī. Whenever this comes about, The shooting pain arises at that very time. Having clearly identified the place of the pain, One should massage it with the ten roots. The oil called Viṣṇu Should always be kept on the head. Through the nose, Vairocana oil, Fully combined with butter and blood. Not tormented by the light of the sun, In a warm house away from people, Holding to the great ocean of union, One should meditate on that yoginī. ya In whose great maṇḍala, ma from the characteristic of the end of the central channel, At the time of moving to the right, The letter daṃ is at all the joints. From the cavity of the opening that causes the pain, At that very instant one strikes with the staff. ṭa While it is becoming completely round, ṣa the pool of the heart and the rest Are to be known at all the junctures as well. For cheating the death that comes with shooting pain, i through the senses in all things, The mind likewise surely abides. The letter called ṣṭi Is thus taught in two verses. There, what is called samādhi: Attain it by training the senses! With a thread of lotus fiber, the jewel And the crown of the head are to be made one. Meditate that the eight consciousnesses abide there: She is the one who flows, the intoxicating one. By sending that thread everywhere, She destroys the fangs of Yama. By that the letter nī is explained. She is the crusher of all shooting pains. Her color is red and blue; The crusher of death goes to awakening. What become her weapons Are those of the yoginī Khaṇḍarohā. In the center of the vajra maṇḍala, The supreme one, made of thirty-seven: oṃ ba byā jra ghra ya ji ma na daṃ a ṣṭī mba ṇī ra ya dha ke rā hūṃ ya hūṃ hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha pha ṭa ṭa phaṭ svā svā hā hā oṃ As the nature of wisdom and means too, She should be meditated upon in the center of the thread. Likewise the month of Āśvina, And the signs of Tulā and Vṛṣabha.

By doing this,
Cheating death is virtuous.
For others, one should do the same for them:
Yamadūtī has been clearly described.
For ransoming the death of others as well,
Hundreds of hundreds of thousands of ways are to be known;
But it is by abiding in total emptiness
And pleasing the supreme guru.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirteenth chapter, on engaging in the application of Yamadaṃṣṭrī, cheating death and the rest, from the Great King of Tantras of the Yoginīs, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas.

Next, the entry of Yamamathanī, By which one goes to the far shore, I shall explain. The yoginīs, having assembled, Asked about its basis: "Why is it called entry? All-pervading Lord, what is meant by the far shore? Most wondrous Teacher, speak! Vajradhara, grant us your kindness!" "Listen! The entry of illusion: The untrue is imputed as true. O goddess, like the net of Indra, So too is the entry of Yamamathanī: Even when it is seen, it is not there. Supreme, like the moon in water, One enters into one's own nature. This belongs to those who hold the yoga of reality. And the reality of timely death: Who can ransom it by deeds? One goes from saṃsāra to the far shore; For the deathless there is no ransom either. At the time of the autumn equinox, Holding the wind In the space maṇḍala of the nose, The time of death is ransomed. The breaths, twenty-one thousand Six hundred in number, Entering fully into the avadhūtī: That is what is called Yamamathanī. The ransom taught by childish scholars Who are deluded is not this. The ransom taught as other than that: This is my own characteristic." Again the goddess was told: "Since the meaning has been shown thus by ya and the rest, Having focused on whatever samādhi, The culmination of the gathering of space-equal and the rest, The continuum, and likewise the great life-breath, Is explained as being like a vajra. Going slowly, slowly, It dissolves into all the joints. Through calm abiding and insight, Awakening is not the domain of death. It is renowned as leading to the vajra ground, The supreme holding of the great state. Through such exalted qualities, The nonvirtue of people is exhausted. Attaining the state of great wealth Is the characteristic of right abandonment. Having made the life-breath move properly, Keep it close at all times; Always cheat the death foretold by the signs of death. For yogins together To meet with yoginīs Is obtained rarely, by the power of merit. Tasting the great joy made with them Is cheating death. Therefore the yogin Should make offerings to the yoginīs. The state of the great Vajradhara Is attained by pleasing them. Whatever fool reviles them Has no supreme accomplishment. From reviling them, One goes to the great hell Avīci. What use is the merit he has made? What use dwelling in fasts, or austerities? Always be respectful to the yoginīs, In every instant, moment and the rest. The goddess has two colors, Embodying existence and nirvāṇa. In the center of the maṇḍala deities She is to be meditated upon, her signs like those of Lāmā. Through the application of the letters of the mantra, With the thirty-seven yoginīs, She is to be generated together with means; Through innate union, the vajra holder. oṃ ba ma jra hā ya dhū ma mrā mandha tha kā nī ra ye ba hūṃ pu hūṃ ṣā pha ya ṭa hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha svā ṭa hā pha oṃ ṭa svā hā Thus one should meditate on the center Of a drop the size of a mustard seed, In the heart and the other wheels, Wherever it is: fully join the mind there! Kanyā, the tortoise, and the month Of Bhādrapada: she dwells in the subsidiary island. Acting for the benefit of beings, She dissolves into that very state, enjoys, and the rest. Born as the nature of the emanation of Vārāhī, She is the crusher of existence. Through the great vajra yoga of the Blessed One, All the deities abide; From the essence of the great wheel-turning king All are emanated; And through perceiving oneself as buddhahood, They are gathered into his very nature. If that gathering does not exist in that way, How could there be emanating? All this is a net of magical illusion: There is no illusion and no emptiness. Its essence is mere total union, Gathered as buddhahood and the rest. The very reality of the Kṛta age, the Tretā age and the rest Is the characteristic of their own nature. What is taught as mere presentation Is the unmistaken essential nature of things. It should be examined by conceptual thought; When it has become nonconceptual, One should not examine it. One will be freed from concept and nonconcept: So the all-seeing ones have taught. Whatever thing anyone sees for an instant, That seeing is like a rabbit's horn. Through this, without cessation, Birth and the rest should be fully understood. Concerning the various scriptures and tantras too, The one who reasons here analyzes. The reasoning that comes from having analyzed, That reasoning is my greatness. For realizing the ground of the mind, It is spoken close to the ear. For the sake of disciples, and out of respect For the guru, hold to this! Nowadays people are short-lived, And I have no other reasoning. In the deceitful age of strife, Intellect is small and merit very small. Therefore the wisdom of the buddhas Is not the domain of those with such merit. Compassion will be cultivated Over a long time: do not engage in conceptual thought! Without having made countless merit, Why speak again of wisdom? What is the mind of awakening? What is a buddha? In this there will be no difference. Because of realizing reality, one is a buddha, And that reality is the bodhisattva himself. Generate the senses as bodhisattvas! Make the objects into buddhahood! Hold the aggregates together with the buddhas! Hold the elements as vajra holders! One will not turn back again into existence. Hold everything as the essence of deathlessness! Abide through the union of compassion! By whatever means, accomplish the needs of others! Apply diligence to all that is inexpressible! The ḍākinīs and the rest, at the end of crushing, All the assemblies of crushers, The gatherings of obstructors, bhūtas and the rest, All who are hostile in the world, Pretas and the rest, and hell beings too, Yama and the other wrathful ones here: She is the crusher of former habitual tendencies." Through the union of all the heroes, Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss, Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata, The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

Thus ends the fourteenth chapter of the Great King of Tantras, the Yoginī Tantra of the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, on generating the entry of Yamamathanī, the characteristics of the rite for cheating death when its time has come, and the nature of the states of a buddha.

Then, from this, the great secret is explained:
The characteristics of perfect meditative absorption.
That by which one abides as glorious Heruka
Is found in none but those who have devotion.
The terrifying and the rest, at the culmination of passion,
Are what is taught as the manifest emanation body.
The Dharma body, the body of complete enjoyment, and the rest
Are here all gathered into one.
First, one should prepare the place in every respect:
A place without people, a mountain, and so on,
Or else a charnel ground; there
Offer torma to the field protectors.
The yogin, after cleansing the mouth and the rest,
Having done the preliminaries, offers welcoming water and the rest.
Focusing on the body of the Blessed One,
As explained for meditation with a painted scroll and the like,
The great fierce one upon a seat of pretas:
Meditating on him brings the benefit of beings.
Wholly and completely, in a single instant,
One should focus on the meditative absorption.
The syllable śrī, the meaning of all that arises,
Is held to be the nature of outer and inner.
These are conventional form,
And whatever has the culmination of bliss that does not enter.
The nonduality of e vaṃ and the rest, too,
Is definitively expressed by the syllable called śrī.
From the syllable he in the heart, in all directions,
Heruka and the others go forth with their own retinues.
From the source of all meditation
Grasping and the rest are generated.
That is the nature of the emptiness of causes;
It is not conceiving them as the nature of a cause.
The intrinsic nature is the selflessness of phenomena;
That is the abiding of the yoga of emptiness.
The arrangement is the selflessness of persons.
The great net of conceptual thought
Is the syllable ru, whose nature is conditions;
That is freedom from the arrangement of a self.
Again, the syllable ka is spoken: whatever knows
That by the self of all without remainder nothing is to be made two;
Because it abides in nothing whatsoever,
Therefore it is called ka.
Things directly perceived are not empty;
They have the nature of mere things.
The goddess abiding in glorious Heruka
Is the one meditated earlier, who pervades all.
In the heart, on a lotus, in the center of a sun,
From the self-arisen seed of wisdom
One should send out light rays of various colors
To fill the whole of the sky.
Having invited the forms of the wisdom ḍākinīs,
The assembly of deities,
In the center of the expanse of the sky
One sets the lord of beings,
And with nectar and the rest, arisen as the nature of the rising moon and sun,
Makes offerings to him.
Having confessed one's misdeeds and the rest,
One should recollect compassion and the rest.
One should meditate on the pure yoga
Whose nature is the intrinsic nature of emptiness.
From the nature of the five seeds
One should emanate a multistoried palace.
Then the causal Vajradhara
Appears clearly upon Mount Meru and a lotus,
White in color, with four faces,
Three eyes, and twelve arms,
Of the nature of union, embracing Prajñā,
Adorned with the ornaments of a youth.
White, green, red,
And yellow, turning from the left.
On the crowns of matted locks of his faces
He bears a crossed vajra and a half moon.
Pressing down Hara and Gaurī together,
He stands firmly with his right leg extended.
Vajra, bell, the hide
Of an elephant, ḍamaru, curved knife,
Axe, and trident likewise,
Khaṭvāṅga, vessel, noose,
And head, in the left and right hands.
A lotus of various colors
With forty-eight petals,
Having the nature of twelve wheels,
With the nature of sixteen doors,
And in the thirty-two delightful charnel grounds.
Curved knife, jewel, vajra,
Lotus, wheel, sword,
Crossed vajra, skull cup,
Head, and skeleton, these,
And noose or hook are to be known:
Wheel within wheel in a garland.
Each one has thirty-six spokes,
With vajra, lotus, and sun.
The causal vajra transformed,
The maṇḍala wheel is complete
With five hundred and four.
Constantly, with the sound of the singing goddesses:

"Lord, when every day beings invite you as their guest,
Why have you entered into emptiness?
Arise, arise! I am the nature of compassion;
Vajradhara, great bliss, make love to me!
Live, live! The aims of others have sunk away;
The world becomes like cattle that have died.
Make love to my blossoming lotus;
In this way make the whole world blissful!
Vajradhara, sport with me, sport!
The inexpressible innate nature is attained.
Since sentient beings and the other worlds are deluded,
How your emptiness becomes meaningless!
You are the maker of all phenomena;
Why do you remain in the innate nature, not going forth?
Make love to me for the supreme aim;
In this way go forth in harmony with the worlds."

Urged on by this song,
Heruka himself fully awakens.
oṃ āḥ kāya vāka citta vajra hūṃ phaṭ hoḥ
When this secret mantra is recited,
One is endowed with the yoga of the instantaneous form:
Oneself, arisen in an instant from the mantra,
With all the heroes and the yoginīs.
Upon Bhairava and Kālarātri,
And upon the great supreme sun at the navel,
His body is in the posture of dancing,
Half black and half green.
With seventy-six arms,
And seventeen faces, each with three eyes,
He wears the hero's crown of matted locks,
With a crossed vajra and a half moon,
His faces baring great fangs.
On the right and the left, always,
Yellow and green are proclaimed;
The west is red, likewise,
And the remaining faces are like black bees,
Baring their fangs, terrifying.
The array of weapons, in due order,
Is to be known on the right and the left.
Two hands hold the elephant hide,
And the others, likewise, the mudrā of the womb.
Vajra, sword, long spear,
And trident, in due order on the right;
Axe, curved knife, arrow,
Stake, shield, hammer,
Wheel, ḍamaru, dagger,
Club, and single-pointed lance,
Conch, copper horn, lynx,
And likewise a peacock's tail,
A crow's feather, a brush,
A fire hearth, a mountain,
A staff, a mirror, a vīṇā,
A foot, a head, a hand, lungs,
Entrails, Rāhu, an iron chain,
A chain, a dubhusa, a fishing net,
A headless body, blazing black oil,
And a form of Bhairava, in due order.

In the left hands: a bell, a long shield, a fang,
A pestle, a noose, a skull cup,
A bow, a khaṭvāṅga, a book,
A small shield with a handle, the threatening finger,
A garland of small bells, an iron chain,
A rock, the dust of a charnel ground,
A bhokaṃ drum, a fresh hide,
A hanging rope of hair,
A stick for stirring the cremation fire,
A quiver, a long shield, a head,
A skeleton, and a hurled ritual weapon likewise,
An eye, a kidney, the nape of a neck,
A peg of tamarisk wood,
A vessel full of seed, a saw,
A needle, a flayed skin,
A cloud pouring rain, and a wooden hook.
The weapons in the hands are seventy-two;
They are to be known in this order.
He is adorned with five heads,
And clothed in the six mudrās;
Likewise a garland of a hundred heads,
Armlets and anklets that ring with sound,
And on his body a garland of hair.
Before him is the great goddess
Vajravārāhī, the same as before,
Moistened by the bliss of wisdom and means
In all the joints of the body.
One should meditate on him blazing with various Herukas,
Radiating out in all directions.
On all the heads a garland of hanging silk
Is to be placed by the one who keeps the observance.
On the lotus petals, in the east and the rest,
And in the spaces between, are the yoginīs,
Twenty-four in number:
Ḍākinī and the others, six by six.
From the east and so on to the northern edge,
Ḍākinī and the others, six likewise;
Beginning from the north to the western edge,
Again Lāmā and the others, six;
From the west and so on to the southern edge,
The family of Khaṇḍarohā and the others;
Beginning from the south to the eastern edge,
Then Rūpiṇī and the others, six.
Ḍākinī and the Embodied One herself,
She Who Nourishes and She Who Transforms Others,
She With a Child and She Who Follows:
These are half black and half blue.
Lāmā and Yogeśvarī,
Bhadrā, the black Kāpālinī,
Jayā and the Encircling One are half green
And half red, in due order.
Khaṇḍarohā and Śmaśānī,
She Who Melts and Kurukullā,
She Who Weeps and She Renowned as the Dancer
Are half red and half yellow.
Rūpiṇī, Bhairavī, She of the Crest,
She of the Topknot, She of Matted Locks, and Raudrī:
These goddesses are half yellow and half black.
Their hand emblems and the rest are like those of Vārāhī.
On the petals in the intermediate directions
Are vessels of the five nectars.
Likewise, with the right leg extended,
They hold garlands of skulls and the rest.
They are to be known as turning to the left:
The ones born in the region above Mount Meru.

Behind that, the vajra wheel,
Dark blue, and in its center:
Ḍākinī, likewise Lāmā,
Khaṇḍarohā and Rūpiṇī,
Pracaṇḍā and Caṇḍākṣī,
Prabhāvatī and Mahānāsā,
Vīramatī and Kharvarī,
Laṅkeśvarī, Drumacchāyā,
Airāvatī, and likewise here
Mahābhairavā is recalled;
Vāyuvegā and Surābhakṣī,
the goddess Śyāmā, Subhadrā,
Hayakarṇā and Khagānanā,
Cakravegā, Khaṇḍarohā,
Śauṇḍinī, Cakravarmiṇī,
Suvīrā, Mahābalā,
Cakravartinī, Mahāvīryā,
Yamī, the Unborn Yamī,
Likewise She Who Shakes, She Who Terrifies,
Caṇḍī and Sarasvatī,
She Who Fulfills Desires and the Great Blazing One.
Their colors are those of their wheel.
They have four arms and one face,
Holding a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
A ḍamaru and a curved knife likewise.
Their hair is loose and they are clothed in the directions.
On their bodies are the five mudrās and the rest,
And they are adorned with garlands of vajras.
On seats of pretas, the great fierce ones,
The yoginīs of all the wheels,
Are endowed with various ornaments,
And likewise endowed with wisdom and means.
Thus the formless realm,
And that too is definitively declared to be a place,
Renowned as the ground Utterly Joyful.
Again the place, the near place, and the rest:
Thus, in the center of the ground as well,
Twelve grounds are held to be.
Thus it is to be known in every case.
Here, in this single element, are all:
The thirty-six that have been explained,
The chief, the nature of all qualities.
Thus the elements have the nature
Of the heroes and the yoginīs.
In the surroundings are the sentient beings,
In the order of the families of the miraculously born.
These, too, are divided among the thirty-six
Lands, land by land, in order.
This is the vajra wheel.

Then what lies outside it is explained.
Again, this heart wheel,
Red and yellow in color, likewise.
In the center of the spokes of the wheel:
Vajradharā, Akṣobhyā,
Vairocanī, Ratneśvarī,
Padmanarteśvarī, Amoghā,
Locanā and Māmakī,
Pāṇḍaravāsinī, Tārā, Rūpavajrā,
Śabdavajrā, likewise Gandhavajrā and Rasavajrā,
Sparśavajrā and Dharmadhātuvajrā,
Kṣitigarbhā and Ākāśagarbhā,
Vajrapāṇī, Lokanāthā,
Sarvanivaraṇaviṣkambhinī, Samantabhadrī,
Ratnapradīpā, Nairātmyā,
Bhṛkuṭī, Parṇaśabarī,
Yamāntakī, Prajñāntakī,
Padmāntakī, Vighnāntakī,
Acalā and Nīladaṇḍā,
Ṭakkirājñī and Mahābalā,
Uṣṇīṣā and Sumbhā.
Their colors are those of their wheel,
And all their characteristics, the faces and the rest,
And everything else, are as in the vajra wheel.
In the near place, the goddesses
Are to be imagined with means like themselves,
And with the right leg extended.
The form realm and the Stainless,
Are held to be the first continent.
The Guide is to be known;
The Lesser Heruka is held to be next;
Third, the Lotus Heruka;
Fourth is held to be the Sky Heruka;
Fifth, the Scattering Wind Heruka;
Sixth, the Earth Heruka;
Seventh, the Blazing Heruka;
Eighth, the Moist Heruka;
Ninth, the Wisdom Heruka;
Tenth, the Mind Heruka;
Eleventh, the Speech Heruka;
And twelfth is held to be the Body Heruka.
These twelve are the great vajra
Herukas; there is no other tantra.
They are the Heruka of the Saṃvara of the three wheels.
Whatever their shapes may be,
Their body colors, from their own wheels,
Are to be indicated by the wise.
This is the heart wheel, the second.

Then, behind that, the wheel called All Qualities,
White and red; in the center of its vajra spokes one places
The thirty-six supreme female messengers:
The brahmin woman, the kṣatriya woman, the vaiśya woman,
The śūdra woman, the caṇḍāla woman, the dog seller,
The ḍombī, the dancer, Kāpālinī,
The fisherwoman and the bamboo worker,
The conch worker and the weaver,
The porter and the carpenter,
The garland maker, the attendant, the dyer,
The cotton spinner, the female messenger,
The latrine sweeper and the painter,
Śauṇḍinī, the woman who tends the garden,
The woman beaten by the king, the butcher woman,
The woman who sells tooth powder,
The goldsmith and the blacksmith,
The jewel stringer, the forest woman,
The barbarian Oḍi woman, the merchant woman,
The stone carver and the field worker,
And the leather worker: these are the yoginīs.
They are the color of the wheel.
All the rest is to be known
As in the heart wheel.
Here it is to be known as the field,
The nature of all the families of the desire realm,
And it is explained as what is called the second continent.
It is the nature of one's own wisdom and means,
And likewise the ground Radiant.
One should worship the guru oneself.
In the mantra that states one's own name,
Homage comes first, as is well known,
And at the end the syllables hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ.
This is to be done in the same way for all the wheels.
Outside that, one places it round,
With two lines, black and blue,
Beautified by doors, porticoes, courtyards,
Nets and half nets.
In the center of the eastern door
Is Kākāsyā, like Ḍākinī;
In the north, Ulūkāsyā;
In the west, Śvānāsyā;
And in the south, Śūkarāsyā;
Their colors are like those of Lāmā and the others, supreme.
In the corners of Agni, Nairṛti, Vāyu,
And Īśāna abide
Yamadāḍhī, Yamadūtī,
Yamadaṃṣṭrī and Yamamathanī, in due order.
Their colors are to be made equal, two by two,
And their faces in corresponding order.
On the ground of the east, the north, the west,
And the south, always,
One makes them black, blue, green, red,
And yellow in color.
Likewise the innate maṇḍala,
Since there are four wheels in sequence:
The Dharma body, the enjoyment body, and the emanation body
Are to be placed at the end, in due order.
The second wheel has three lines,
The third has four lines,
And the fourth has the nature of five lines.
The charnel grounds lie all around in order,
And the ground outside and so on
Is held to be made in a round shape.
The wheel, with the nature of five lines,
Is four cornered and blazes all around.
There the charnel grounds of the innate wheel
Are to be explained: Caṇḍogra,
Gahvara, and likewise
Jvālākula and Karaṅkin, the terrible ones,
Abide in the directions of the east and the rest,
Turning to the left.
Aṭṭaṭṭahāsa is in Īśāna's quarter,
Śrīvana in Agni's,
Ghorāndhakāra in Nairṛti's,
And in Vāyu's, Kilikilārava, resounding.
The forms in the fierce charnel grounds
Are vetālas, bhūtas, and the cries of jackals;
They abide in this order.
In the eight charnel grounds as well
Are trees, guardians of the directions, and nāga lords.
The trees: Śirīṣa, Kaṅkali,
The Cūta tree and the Nyagrodha,
Likewise Karañjaka itself,
Latāparkaṭi and Pithava.
The guardians: Indra, Kubera likewise,
Varuṇa, and the Lord of Yama's realm,
Then Īśāna and Agni,
The lord of rākṣasas, and the lord of wind.
The nāgas: Vāsuki and Takṣaka likewise,
Balacara and Padma itself,
Mahāpadma and Huluhulu,
Kulika and Śaṅkhapāla.
The Thundering, the Rumbling,
The Fierce, the Swirling, the Dense likewise,
The Full, the Rain Pouring, and the Violent:
These are the lords of the clouds.
Here, in all the charnel grounds,
One places a vajra garland.
Everything here is likewise to be done;
It is held to be outer and inner.
This is the third, the wheel of qualities.

Then, outside, the wheel of space,
Like a blue lotus;
In the center of its thirty-six spokes
Are these sky-going yoginīs:
The kinnarī, the gandharvī,
Huṭukā, likewise Vāṭavā,
The vīṇā player, the flute player, Mukundā,
The clay drum player, and surely Gaggarī,
The cymbal player and Śelendrikā,
The singer, Karaḍā, and the supreme Tamaḍā,
The dancer, the coquette, and Ṭakkā,
Tālī, Śaraṇā, the great drum player,
Maudrī, the string player, and Marū,
Nalavā, and likewise Ḍhambakī,
Ḍamarū and likewise Ḍuṇḍūkī,
The copper horn player and likewise the horn trumpet player,
Bhukī, the bell player, and the small bell player,
The jingle player, and likewise Ḍakolikā,
The conch player, and likewise Svaravatī:
The supreme sovereign ladies of the retinue.
Their colors are various and bright,
Or else they are the color of the wheel.
They abide in the near field;
The continent is the third, supreme.
They have come in the family of the sky-goers
And abide perfectly in this continent.
The ground is Radiating Light,
And they are the women of the field, in their own places and the rest.
Or else, the ḍamaru and the khaṭvāṅga
Are here set aside once more,
And each, in her own way, bears her own sign
As she wishes.
On the crowns of all the wheels
Is placed their own lord.
Here, of the nature of wisdom and means,
Of the nature of Kulika, they are women of the family.
Their ornaments and the rest are many;
The rest is to be done as before.
In all the wheels they have three eyes,
And they are followers clothed in the directions.
This is the wheel of space, the first.

Then, outside, the wheel of wind,
Blue and of various colors.
In the center of the vajra spokes one places
The yoginīs in due order.
Their other names, from Ākāśagarbha,
Are to be known by the intelligent:
The garuḍī, the goose, the speckled one,
The crow, the bya gar bird, the partridge,
The peahen, She of the Copper Crest,
Guḍabulī, the gentle one,
The dove and the great raven,
Gaḍinī and the burning owl,
The parrot, the mynah, the laughing one,
The vulture, the owl, the sparrow,
The woodpecker and the ruddy duck,
Vṛkṣaraṇī, the kakkapa
Bird, the cat,
The willow throat and Sārī,
The hawk, She of the Saffron Tongue,
Vāṭirī, Kāṃjaṃghā,
Hormo, and Kaṃkā likewise,
Datnarī, and the Enemy of Deer.
Thus the wheel of the yoginīs.
Their color is like that of the ground of the wheel,
Or else it is to be known from their own activities.
Their hands and the rest are likewise as before.
All are of the nature of wisdom and means.
They are the supreme ones abiding in the meeting place,
To be known as on the ground Difficult to Purify,
And held to be of the fourth continent.
This wheel is of the egg-born,
With the divisions of the three qualities;
The third, with the nature of the three,
Is the wheel of the heat-and-moisture-born, the embodied;
The fourth is called the womb-born.
They are held to have the nature of the three wheels.
This is the wheel of wind, the second.

Then what lies outside is to be explained:
The supreme wheel that bears the name of earth,
Yellow in its nature,
Adorned with thirty-six spokes.
The yoginīs are thirty-six,
The earth-going ones, in due order:
The lioness, the tigress, Bhimbhā, the hare,
The elephant, the deer, the cat,
The cow, the buffalo, the mare,
The jackal, the rhinoceros, the female yak,
The mouse, the she-ass, Bhiḍī,
The she-goat and the ewe, in order,
The bitch, the sow, the black bear,
Ḍanaḍarī, Maujñākā,
The brown bear and the coquette,
The forest woman and the great hound,
The great raven, Khasgomā,
The snake, the monkey, the camel,
The weasel, the lizard, and the dove:
The supreme ones who dwell in the villages.
Thus they are the color of the wheel,
Or else each has her own color.
The goddesses, of the nature of wisdom and means,
Dwell in the near meeting place,
On the ground Manifest,
Which is the perfection of wisdom,
And they abide in the fifth continent.
Their weapons and the rest are as before.
Their bodies are in the form of women,
And their faces are held to be of their own kind.
At the doors of the east, the north, the west,
And the south are to be known
Brahmāṇī, Maheśvarī,
Kaumārī, and likewise Vaiṣṇavī;
Vārāhī, Indrāṇī, Caṇḍī,
And Mahālakṣmī are in the corners.
Those at the doors, in form and sign,
Are known to be like Ḍākinī and the others;
In the corners, like the four, Dāḍhī and the rest.
Here are to be known three lines:
The dharmas of body, speech and mind.
The wheel of Dharma is most beautiful.
The charnel grounds are to be described:
In the center of the fire wheel,
Killing, the Terrifier of All,
Great Fear, the Terrifier,
And likewise the fierce charnel ground,
Are to be arranged turning to the left;
The Expeller and the Hater,
The Muter, and again the Paralyzer,
Are in the corners of Īśāna and the rest.
The trees are to be placed in order:
The areca, the walnut, the coconut,
The pomegranate, the bilva tree, then
The myrobalan and the vijñārakṣa tree,
To be arranged in due order.
Indrāṇī, Yamī, Vāruṇī,
Yakṣiṇī, Bhūtī, the ṛṣi woman,
The rākṣasī, and the wife of Vāyu:
These world protectresses are to be placed.
All the nāginīs and rākṣasīs
Are to be placed here in every way.
Thus, having explained the retinue of the maṇḍala,
One places every excellence.
With a body color that accords with the activity,
One should meditate there on the chief.
This is the third wheel, the earth maṇḍala, with its two stages.

Then outside, here once again,
I shall explain the fire wheel,
Red in color, greatly blazing.
On its thirty-six supreme spokes
The yoginīs are again to be arranged,
Born from the families of the gods and the rest:
Goddesses, nāginīs, yakṣiṇīs,
And bhūtīs, in mere essence.
What is it that all this has spoken of?
I shall explain those born of the family of the gods:
The mother, the wife, the sister,
The daughter, the sister's daughter,
The father's sister,
The wife of the maternal uncle,
The wife's sister,
Her father's mother,
The wife's grandmother,
The mother's mother, the kinswoman,
The daughter of the mother's sibling,
The sibling of one's own grandmother,
The sibling's daughter and her daughter,
The father's mother and the grandmother,
The wife of the paternal uncle,
The wife of the daughter's son,
Again the wife's sibling,
The daughter of one's own father's sister,
Born of her own family,
The sibling's wife and daughter,
The son's own wife,
The mother of the daughter's husband,
The son's own mother-in-law,
And the daughter's daughter, as is rightly explained:
These are the thirty-six messengers.
They are rightly known to be red in color,
Their weapons and the rest as before.
The ground is Gone Far,
And they abide in the sixth continent.
They are always to be known as in the assembly,
With the essential nature of wisdom and means.
In the wheel of power, always,
They are to be known as lovely women like oneself.
Everywhere, with vajra garlands and the rest,
Reversing the order of arising,
With the left and the right hands
Offerings are made here.
All the flesh of livestock
Is to be offered everywhere as food.
Drinks of various kinds are offered,
And likewise various things to lick.
Rising each from her own seat,
They worship the guru Vajradhara;
Then, coming back again,
To the twelve offerings of the wheel.
This is the first, the fire wheel, the supplementary layer of the enjoyment body.

Then what lies outside that is explained:
The water wheel, of great miraculous power,
White in color; on its thirty-six spokes
One meditates on the yoginīs in order:
The makarī, the turtle, the fish,
The frog, the tortoise, the otter,
The black scorpion, Gaggarī, Śrīlī,
She of the water cave, She with an insect's face,
Chagapā, Kakarū, Susā,
The water rat, She with an ant's face,
The water woman and the wild one,
The elephant, the tigress, the jackal,
The water snake, the conch, the cowrie,
The mother of pearl, the jewel, Jiṅgurā,
Lisā, Dudurā, Karṇāṭā,
Phaṭakā, Dāvakā, Krimā,
Juṣā, Juṣī, the blowfly, and Kalā:
They are the supreme chief goddesses.
Thus their colors by nature
Are white, or each born of her own family.
Their faces are in their own forms,
In the order of the yoginīs.
This is the near assembly,
And the ground is Immovable, supreme.
They are to be known as abiding in the seventh continent;
Their weapons are as before.
Or else the people of Pūrṇagiri and the rest
Are the thirty-six deities.
In those places and the rest, in order,
Actual women are to be properly set.
By them this Jambudvīpa
Is adorned with twelve segments.
In the ocean called Salty,
The beings that belong there
Are to be thought of as born of heat and moisture,
Born from union, or born from the womb.
This wheel is of the heat-and-moisture-born;
The fire wheel is of the womb-born.
It is complete with all characteristics,
Itself the nature of wisdom and means.
This is the water wheel, the second.

Then what lies outside that is explained:
On every side, the wheel of wisdom.

It has thirty-six spokes of various colors,
With the family of asura women in order:
Tilottamā and Supreme Bliss,
The Daughter of the Gods, Great Joy,
Joy, and Renowned Joy, Padminī,
Śaṅkhinī, Citriṇī, Hastinī,
Great Form and Beautiful Form,
Lover of Light, the Graceful, the Blissful,
Lover of Flowers, White Lotus,
Blue Lotus and the Lovely,
Passion and Great Passion,
Renowned Play and Great Play,
Intoxicated and Delighting in Intoxication,
Desire and Great Desire,
Born of Bliss and Possessed of Bliss,
Supremely Agreeable and Loving,
Fortunate and Fair Fortune,
the Lowly and the Shrinking,
And she who is renowned as Born Form:
These are the supreme chief yoginīs.
All are of the nature of wisdom and means,
Of various kinds and colors likewise.
Their hands and weapons are to be known as before.
The ground is Good Intelligence, likewise,
And the charnel ground Appearing as Jewels,
The maker of self-arisen bliss.
Therefore the enjoyment body
And the third wheel of the maṇḍala,
Through the purity of the four joys,
Have four lines all around.
At the four doors, in the east and the rest,
The yoginīs in due order,
Gaurī, Caurī, Vetālī,
And Ghasmarī, are to be arranged.
The four goddesses dwelling in the corners,
Pukkasī, likewise Śavarī,
Caṇḍālī and Ḍombinī,
Are always to be known in order, as before.
The outer wheel of charnel grounds
Is in the center of the water maṇḍala:
Death of Children, Eater of Leftovers,
Fierce Battle, and All Fangs,
In the east and the rest, turning to the left;
Thus they are to be imagined with great love.
Smoke, black darkness, and fire,
The sound of hā hā, and the great sound.
The trees are sandalwood and camphor,
Nutmeg and cardamom,
Nāgakesara and campaka,
Kunduru and the deodar.
The guardians of the directions are to be shown:
The Sun, the Moon, the Son of the Earth,
The one born of the Hare-Marked, the Guru himself,
Śukra, likewise the Son of the Sun,
And the Asura Head are to be known;
They are to be given fierce faces.
Blazing swords, knives,
Long spears, razors, likewise rocks,
Lightning, and a rain of various weapons
Are to be made in the center of the charnel grounds.
The sword, the eye ointment, the pill,
The foot ointment, the elixir,
The sandals, mercury, and the underworld:
These accomplishments are to be achieved by the wise.
This is the wheel of wisdom, the nature of the three wheels of the maṇḍala of the enjoyment body.

Then what lies outside that is explained:
This clear wheel of mind,
Black in color, with thirty-six spokes,
All supreme ones born of their own nature:
The nāginī, the yakṣiṇī, the bhūtī,
The pretī, the hell woman, Avīcī,
She Who Falls and the Unremitting,
The Kumbhī, and likewise Yamī,
Black Thread and Burning Embers,
Heat and Intense Heat,
Wailing and Great Wailing,
She Who Boils in Black Oil, She Who Crushes Between Two Mountains,
Hatred, Delusion, Jealousy,
Lustful Intoxication, Avarice, She of the Thread,
She of Counting, She of the Forest of Swords herself,
the Many, and Famine,
She of the Wilderness of Disease, She of Weapons,
She of the Wilderness of Water,
Sword Nails, Vaitaraṇī,
Razor Teeth, She of the Wheel,
And the kumbhāṇḍī: the great goddesses.
Their color is like that of the wheel.
Their hands and weapons are as before,
And they are to be known as having their own forms.
There is the near charnel ground,
And then the ground is Cloud of Dharma.
It is the pure nature of the mind,
Held to be the all-pervading, possessed of the wheel.
Free of inherent nature, selfless,
They dwell on the ground Possessed of the Wheel.
The wheels in due order
Are all to be known in this way.
The doorkeepers of them all
In the four places are twelve;
Thus sixteen are to be known.
Emptiness belongs to the wise;
Because wisdom is the essence of consciousness,
The charnel ground is the Blessed One himself.
In this emanation wheel
It is by the names of the nature of the three.
Outside, all of this other
Is to be explained as two wheels.
This is the first of the wheel of mind.

Then, outside, another:
The wheel of speech is to be explained,
Red, the color of madder,
Chief, with the nature of thirty-six spokes:
She Who Worships, She Who Eats, She Who Sleeps,
The Lazy One, She Who Meditates with a Mind on Dharma,
She Whose Mind Is on the Household, She Whose Mind Is on Women,
She Whose Mind Is on Wealth, She Parted from Union,
