Then in the seven segments of the second,
gri ya hoḥ gri bha ga pa ya
bā na bi phaṭ: write these in order.
In the third, on the sides of the middle,
One should place three on each side:
Place phaṭ hṇa hūṃ hūṃ hṇā.
In the fourth, from the center in every
Direction leaving out one each,
And in the middle of the two doors,
The right and the left, two syllables each:
One should place pa ya dyā rā.
What is to be set out in the fifth row
Is three on each side of the middle:
Set out hūṃ pa ya hūṃ hūṃ gri su.
These, in their order just as here,
Are to be known for the sixth row:
hṇa an hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ, and likewise
phaṭ ja hūṃ phaṭ, in their proper order.
All the mantras are to be placed;
This is explained for the seventh segments.
Leaving out the doors, in order,
One should place hūṃ hūṃ mbha hūṃ.
This is the greatest of great yantras.
In the five central segments,
And likewise in the four doors,
The name of the one to be subdued is inserted,
Together with the mantra of a hundred feet.
The vetāla that comes to fight
Should be seized by the wise one.
The yantra goes into their belly;
It will succeed, of this there is no doubt.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
The Tathāgata Vajraḍāka,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-fourth chapter, called "The Nature of the Yantra Wheel of the Rite of Characteristics Distinguished by the Application of Śauṇḍinī," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next, the samādhi of Cakravarmiṇī
I shall explain clearly:
Through the application of Cakravarmiṇī,
It is the rite of binding into muteness.
The site of Suvarṇadvīpa, the Golden Isle,
And then mindfulness, the limb of awakening;
The yantra in the form of a rākṣasa
Is employed in another rite.
On the ground of the crown and the soles
The wise one should perform it.
In the great battle of the gods and the asuras
They fell speechless; so it enters among humans.
At that time the great form
Was beheld through the samādhi of the wheel.
What is explained by the two syllables
Is the binding of the moving mind.
The suppressed phenomenon of consciousness:
Make it dissolve into the midst of form.
The sublime Dharma taught in the tantra
Is here explained by two mudrās.
Likewise, through the former conduct as well,
One will practice the mudrā of union.
In the chapter of all the instructions,
By meditating on what has that as its cause,
Gradually, all of this, profoundly,
One purifies by oneself.
Space, spaceless, great space,
Utter space, not of space:
It descends into the upward-facing channel.
As long as the line is not seen,
So long one obtains it without interruption.
How could the bliss born of joy not descend as well?
Through the mingling of entity and nonentity
Beings arise on every side.
Nonentity is not of the nature of form;
Bliss is born from all nonentities.
One is carried to abide on the path;
Whatever is there, just as it is, so it is.
Wisdom abides like one who is mute;
The binding is born of one's own mudrā.
Through this one goes into muteness,
And like an enemy it pierces all.
If even Vajradhara is struck mute,
What need is there to speak of petty beings?
Through awareness the agent does everything;
Another agent is not possible.
Sumanā and the person itself:
From the instant one comes to know selflessness,
One then abides in the Vajrayāna;
Intrinsic nature is held to be selfless.
Expression, and the expression of selflessness too,
Do not arise through yogic seeing.
The great yoga that rises from that
Is the syllable hṇa, not through other entities.
The churning and churning of the two
Is first from the crown to the end of the feet.
Through joining the two together,
The central arrow is born of mantra.
What is aimed at by the aiming of the target:
There is neither target nor one who aims.
The thread, like a fine fiber,
The yogin follows to the final place.
Arrow and bow are not seen;
Wherever it is released, let it go.
Then all living beings become mute;
Of this there is no doubt.
One meditates on the wheel of the maṇḍala,
And in its center sets this out:
The goddess Cakravarmiṇī,
With color and shape as before,
Arisen from the mantra syllables,
Supreme, whose nature is wisdom and skillful means.
oṃ sa ba rba ndha bi na ka ka la rā pa ya bha hūṃ ñja hūṃ na pha ka ṭa rā pha ya ṭa hūṃ svā hūṃ hā phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
oṃ ca kra barmi ṇī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Thus, with the mantra as the very nature of the application,
One should accomplish the muteness of consciousness.
Having drawn the yantra described before,
One should set out this garland of secret mantra.
First is Vairocana;
In the second, place van;
The syllable bi should be placed in the third;
In the fourth, the syllable hūṃ;
In the fifth, the syllable bya;
In the sixth segment, place rā;
In the seventh, the syllable gri;
In the eighth, the syllable ga;
In the ninth, place the syllable mbha;
In the tenth, set out the syllable hṇa again;
The syllable gri is in the eleventh;
The syllable hūṃ should be set in the twelfth;
In the thirteenth, the syllable gri;
In the fourteenth, the syllable ja;
In the fifteenth, the syllable bha;
Set out the two pa ya in the sixteenth;
In the seventeenth, the syllable su;
Set out the syllable pha in the eighteenth;
In the one less than twenty, place hūṃ;
In the twentieth, the syllable ṭa;
In the twenty-first, the syllable hūṃ;
In the twenty-second there is hūṃ;
In the twenty-third, the syllable gri;
The syllable hūṃ is in the twenty-fourth;
In the twenty-sixth, place the syllable ṭa;
In the twenty-seventh, the syllable pha;
The syllable hūṃ is in the twenty-eighth;
The syllable hoḥ in the twenty-ninth;
In the thirtieth, place the syllable hṇā;
In the thirty-first, set out hṇa;
Place the syllable hūṃ in the thirty-second;
Here in the thirty-third, the syllable mbha;
In the thirty-fourth, the syllable hūṃ,
And that same one in the thirty-fifth;
In the thirty-sixth, place the syllable yi;
The syllable hṇā is in the thirty-seventh;
In the thirty-eighth, the two pa ya;
In the one less than forty, hūṃ;
In the fortieth, place hūṃ;
In the forty-first, the syllable bha;
Place the syllable hūṃ in the forty-second;
In the forty-third, oṃ;
The two syllables ya na are to be placed
In the forty-fourth segment;
In the forty-fifth, ā na;
In the forty-sixth, likewise phaṭ;
In the forty-seventh, oṃ;
In the forty-eighth, phaṭ;
In the forty-ninth, place su.
All are in the order just as given.

In the central segment, the name of the one to be subdued
Is inserted within ka ra ka ra and the rest.
Having gathered it all and understood it,
To bind someone into muteness,
At a doorway over which rats pass,
Hide the yantra together with an ox's hoof.
Having written the syllables in the avadhūtī,
Place the name firmly in its center.
Having made rākṣasas in all the directions,
One accomplishes the yantra.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
The Tathāgata Vajraḍāka,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-fifth chapter, called "The Wheel of the Yantra in the Form of a Rākṣasa: The Characteristics of the Rite of Applying Cakravarmiṇī to Cause Muteness, the Meditation on the Wheel, and the Yoga Nondual with the Hero," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next I shall clearly explain the rite Of Suvīrā, which brings pacification. It is to be done through those applications. She dwells perfectly in Nagara; She is equanimity, the limb of awakening; The wheel of the deities, of great miraculous power. At the site of the forehead one should meditate On the goddess, the mistress of beings. From the perfect union of the two organs The great channel is filled with bliss. From its moving up to the end of the twelve, Here the seed does not stir. The senses and all the rest Will attain freedom from attachment. The great yoga is the bliss of sameness, Yet again it has no intrinsic nature of bliss. Free from the stains of attachment, aversion and darkness, One attains the great far shore. Through that joy One plays with the male in nonduality. There is nothing other than the three vehicles. At all times they watch over them: Walking, lying down and eating Are given up by accomplishing yoga. Through amorous delight, play and games, And through singing songs, there is no defilement. Therefore it is equanimity, the limb of awakening, Because it has left behind every state. When it is done in this way for pacification, It is accomplished through an unfailing gaze. Whatever sentient beings, The sick and the rest, one looks upon, For each and every one of those beings It becomes a rite of pacification. If even buddhahood comes swiftly, What need is there to speak of minor rites? In the center of the assembly of yoginīs One should meditate on Suvīrā. The goddess is always white in color; The rest is supreme, as before. Through the application of the garland of secret mantra Wisdom and skillful means arise. Through the rites of torma and fire offering The intelligent one, with pacifying substances, Curd, butter, milk and foods, Should here make offerings of oblations. With camphor, sandalwood and the like One should draw the yantra as before. oṃ pa mu ra ṇḍā kṝ ba ta lī ya la ntra ga ma na ntra śa nā rī śa rā nā ya ya hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ svā svā hā hā oṃ su bī rī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā These mantra syllables are the seeds, The cause of arising and of abiding. All the seeds of wisdom The mantrin places at the forehead and the rest. From the time of the yogins' play They are generated and they cease. In the forty-nine segments One should set out this garland of mantra. In the eight segments outside the central one, In the order of the directions and the intermediate directions, Are the four seeds of Vairocana And the four syllables hūṃ. Outside that, in sixteen segments, In the order of the directions and the intermediate directions, One should set out eight syllables hūṃ, And then the words grihṇa grihṇa; sumbha ni goes in the remaining segments. Outside that, in the twenty-four, mbha gri hṇā pa ya gri hṇā pa ya Is to be written here; The words ā na ya ā na ya hoḥ, bha ga bān bidyā rā ja, And then the syllable phaṭ should be written. In the central segment, the one to be accomplished Is inserted within the mantra of Sumbha. One should write it on the mouth of the deity's image. In this way the deity's yantra Should be placed in the position of the effigy. One should know all the conduct thoroughly; At any other time it will not succeed. At the time of the drop and the sound, Through the stability of the shape of the wind, If buddhas and the minds of awakening Pass into peace, into nirvāṇa, What need is there to speak again Of pacifying works for those of the world upon the earth? The lord of yogins who does this Is worshiped by gods and asuras. If he too attains buddhahood itself, What need to speak of those who attend on him? By the world and the great ones, the buddhas and the rest, He is worthy of homage and worthy of offerings. Through meditative concentration, mantra recitation, Fire offering, sacrifice (yāga) and the rest, supremely, Pacification and the mind of awakening Will be accomplished; of this there is no doubt. By reciting the root mantra All the rites will be accomplished. Through the forty-eight feet Omniscience arises in this very life. One will see Vajravārāhī, Nondual with glorious Heruka. She grants his supreme attainment: Dissolving into one's own wheel-turning monarch. Those who accomplish the mudrā Become supremely marvelous. Dwelling together with the ḍākinīs, One attains it in site after site. If one does not know Heruka's mantra and yantra And all the rites, That is pointless, utter weariness: One will wander in the six destinies, And for hundreds of koṭis of births All the beings of the world are not freed. Abiding in the state of glorious Heruka, One is freed by the power of mantra. With great love one should know how to approach Vārāhī and the other yoginīs. The aspect of the bliss of union Is neither woman nor man nor neuter; As a mere entity, to sentient beings, The tathāgatas have taught it. From the union of all the heroes, Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss, The Tathāgata Vajraḍāka, The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-sixth chapter, called "The Collection of Rites of the Sumbha Mantra, and the Meditation on the Wheel of the Yantra: The Characteristics of the Rite of Applying Suvīrā's Pacifying Activity," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the goddess, having made offerings,
Asked in these words:
"The great rite of protecting all,
O all-pervading Lord, I do not know."

"O Vārāhī, goddess who holds the lotus of divine substance, Listen! I shall explain it fully. Through the application of Mahābalā The site of Sindhu is accomplished. As the very nature of right view One should know the supreme mātṛkā. In this way one should ascertain that reality And enter into the supreme meditation. In the delightful town of the wheel of mātṛkās One should meditate on the chief lady of the heroes. Through the reasoning of innate awakening, Whatever is indicated is of her nature. First is hā, as in the progression: This is the characteristic of the fierce application. Having fully focused on all the yoga, The youthful one rising from the root of the aggregates Is held in the skull at the crown of the head. Reaching the aperture of Oḍḍiyāna And attaining the site of the navel, It is held by the seeds of awakening. So that it may be seized by the night, It is made full with all the elements. Having reached the path of the lalanā, It is truly known in its former site. With the element melted by the fire One should fill all the hollows of the channels. In all the world realms, In a single instant, one looks and sees. In the middle of the course of the moon One should gaze at what hangs at the door. Having understood and accomplished the supreme union, One should contemplate oneself as having its nature. Into the aperture that reaches the ūrṇā between the brows One should insert the tongue of the wind. Through the stages of meditation of the yogas It is to be known as right view. One should meditate on the wisdom yoginī, Supreme, the embodiment of the thirty-seven, Arisen from the syllables of the mantra, Supreme, the embodiment of wisdom and skillful means. oṃ du ma ṣa hā ṭā ra nāṃ kṣā bi ka dā rā la ya ne hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ pha pha ṭa ṭa pha pha ṭa ṭa svā svā hā hā jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ oṃ ma hā ba le hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā From this mantra the great goddess, A body of wisdom, perfectly arises. One should always draw the yantra of awakening, Square, together with a circle. In its forty-nine segments The four secret mantras, properly, Should be written, one in each segment. In five segments is the practitioner, Inserted within gha gha and the rest, In the hand of the bodhisattva; It is to be written with bezoar, Or else with great bezoar. All the yantras, on birch bark, Are written according to the particular rite. On cloth from a great battlefield All the rites are to be performed." From the union of all the heroes, Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss, The Tathāgata Vajraḍāka, The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-seventh chapter, called "The Characteristics of the Rite of the Instruction on Meditating on the Bodhisattva Yantra, the Rite of the Kīla Mantra, the Great Protection through the Application of Mahābalā," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next I shall explain all the supreme rites
As they arise for all sentient beings.
In the application of Cakravartinī
One should accomplish them at the site of Maru.
Right thought abides
As a factor of awakening.
Increase is to be accomplished; after that
The wise one performs the rite.
By kindling Caṇḍī at the navel
All the joints are burned;
Having burned the eyes and the rest,
Through the burning, drops drip from the palate.
It goes forth in every direction,
A net of lotus fiber threads.
Piercing as it goes, it tastes
The seventy-two thousand.
The wisdom of dullness and torpor,
Although it does not go, will come.
The yogin of this mudrā
Accomplishes it through a year's practice.
Fully endowed with that and with this,
He will perform all the rites.
"Joy in all the worlds":
So it is called; one goes forth as its very nature.
If one carries that conceptual thought along,
One will not go a single step.
Having become the essence of all the joints,
One will see that as one's own seat.
Because of this, unafraid of death,
One abides fully in another's seat.
Whatever rites they look to,
All of those they perform.
Knowing it from the time of death:
That is held to be the seat of yoga.
Omniscient and accomplishing all the rites,
Because it is the supreme secret seat.
The very application of right thought
Becomes a factor of awakening.
The flesh of cattle, horses, humans and dogs,
Elephants, buffaloes and donkeys,
Camels, mice and cats,
Pigs and monkeys,
Mixed with something sour:
One who desires accomplishments should make a fire offering with it.
If one attains the natural accomplishment,
Why not the eight accomplishments as well?
The sword, eye ointment and foot salve,
Pills, mercury and boots,
The elixir and the underworld,
And summoning too, are accomplished.
For the accomplishment of raising a vetāla,
With the milk of a black bitch
And a porridge of black māṣa beans,
On the night of the fourteenth, the pretas' night,
Or on the eighth day, the mantrin
Likewise makes a fire offering in the mouth of a corpse.
Having carefully taken mustard seed oil from the charnel ground
And melted butter,
With that one should anoint the hero,
And that same night make the full proclamation.
The great preta rises
And grinds to dust upon the ground of the great power.
One should never be afraid of it,
For it is produced through the application of one's own conditions.
Sesame or sesame oil, or anything else,
Whatever is suitable, having taken it,
The yogins give it for the sake of beings,
And so it goes everywhere.
Tārā, Cundā and Marīcī,
Bhṛkuṭī and Parṇaśabarī,
And the great wrathful ones, with Yamāntaka and the rest:
Meditation and mantra recitation
A hundred thousand times destroys wrongdoing,
And ten million times brings one to meet them.
They grant whatever accomplishment is desired,
And one enters the stream.
Or else one knows poetry
And expounds the meaning of scripture.
One knows everything, the languages of all,
And comes to know what is unseen.
Subduing and caring for all,
One does everything in accord with the Dharma.
Tārā and the other supreme yoginīs
Are to be known as other forms of oneself.
As the form that gathers all of these,
Meditate on Cakravartinī.
She holds manifold forms;
Through them she is the Cakravartinī.

oṃ tā re tuttva re tu re svā hā
oṃ cunde svā hā
oṃ mā ri cyai svā hā
oṃ bhrī ku ṭī svā hā
oṃ parṇṇa śa ba rī svā hā
In this way the yantras of all of them
Are also made with the syllables of the mantras.
As the cause of generation and of abiding
They should be accomplished by the appropriate method.
Enveloped all around by garlands of light rays,
They should be held in the wheel of the heart.
This destroys the fear of death
And brings the attainment of freedom from aging and death.
The goddess Cakravartinī,
Supreme embodiment of wisdom and skillful means,
With her own color and shape,
Arises perfectly from the mantra syllables.
oṃ kro dha dharmo sphā dha ra ya sphā ka ra rā ṇā ya ya hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ pha pha ṭa ṭa pha pha ṭa ṭa svā svā hā hā
oṃ cakra bartti nī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Thus arisen perfectly from the mantra
Is she who turns the wheel of the ḍākinīs.
One should draw the asura yantra:
In its forty-nine segments
The mantras of the deities, as they are,
With the name of the one to be subdued inserted,
And the heart mantra of Vārāhī as well,
Are written in each of the segments.
On the outside, with all the mantras,
One should always encircle it here.
On cloth from a battlefield,
Having drawn it fully with all the substances,
The yantra for all the rites
Should be placed in the mouth of an asura.
Applying it through the days and nights of a year,
One should meditate at each of the junctures of the sessions.
One should draw the yantra of Yamāri:
In its seventeen segments,
Placing sixteen on the outside,
One should set out this mantra: hrīḥ ṣṭrī
ya ma me da kṣe rā jā sa do
ru ṇa yo ni rā ya ccha ni.
Having written the great mantra in this way,
One should accomplish all the rites.
Through the chief of each syllable
The rites too are seventeen.
Having inserted the name of the one to be subdued,
The wise should write the heart mantras of Yama
On the outside, with the syllables
In reverse order.
It utterly destroys fears, the royal clans,
And all enemies.
Further, for binding the clay drum,
The all-pervading Lord's own mantra is extracted.
On ground that is level and delightful,
Smeared with sandalwood and the like,
The practitioner also does this:
With nine lines, well rounded,
In the forty-nine segments,
At the heads of one, two, three and four,
In forward order and in reverse,
One should set down the syllables of the mantra.
Then the mantrin properly extracts
The mantra of Sumbharāja.
It is to be known as all the junctures;
It has not been taught in other tantras.
Its syllables are in the channels,
And the junctures of each one of them,
The junctures of the forty-nine,
Have the sum of space, time and fire.
Whatever is fully explained as a dharma,
Through meditative equipoise, those are its junctures.
Wherever a single syllable is taken,
There are all the syllables.
That they are extracted by the steps of calculation
Is taught in the Hundred Thousand Saṃvara.
The yantra whose nature is outer and inner
Accomplishes everything for the yogins.
Secret mantra should be accomplished through bliss,
And the yantra above all.
That one will attain accomplishment through it,
Here there can be no doubt.
It has the form of support and supported,
Name and site in their respective order.
The site is in the form of the support;
The name of the supported is the yoginī.
Their equal absorption
Is declared to be for all the rites.
Therefore the yoginī tantra
Is the supported, the domain of skillful means.
Because the ground is the support,
Therefore skillful means is the yoginī.
Or else, everything is skillful means;
Or else, everything is wisdom.
By those who are free of wisdom and means
The body of a buddha passes beyond sorrow.
This supreme vehicle, when separated from wisdom,
Makes everything go into existence.
Whatever dharmas are existence and nirvāṇa,
Through abandoning them there is no dharma.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
The Tathāgata Vajraḍāka,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-eighth chapter, called "The Characteristics of the Rite Summarizing the Mantra of the Clay Drum, and the Path of Meditation on the Yantra Wheels that Accomplish Various Rites, Beginning with the Application of Cakravartinī," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next, the other supreme chief,
The increase of Cakravartinī and the rest,
The rite that grants every benefit,
I shall explain in brief.
Through the application of Mahāvīryā,
She dwells fully in Kulutā.
The factor of awakening is right speech;
The yantra has a human form.
Relying fully on the pendant,
One attains inconceivable awakening.
It is the chief of all the rites,
The self-arisen great seal.
It arises perfectly from all the elements;
It is called the pendant.
For the twenty-four heroes,
Each one has eight aspects.
In all the elements too it is to be known:
The eye, the aperture and the moon.
Each of all the heroes, individually,
Will also be named by his own name.
In the thirty-seven maṇḍalas
One should meditate on each of the heroes.
The eight elements in all of them
Are also to be marked as threefold.
Through the summary of the names of the applications,
Such as the skull fragment and the rest,
One should know the name of Mahāvīryā
As abiding in Vārāhī and the others.
By gently striking the elements
One should wake from sleep.
Just as the sword of a hero
Pierces one of great strength,
So the sword of the pendant
Pierces all the elements.
Having understood the summary of hā,
Abide at all the junctures.
The heroes generated from that
Number tens of millions, hundreds of thousands and thousands.

Having the nature of the seed of awakening,
The yoginī is known as its family.
Sealing all the elements,
She arises perfectly at the time of emergence.
One should know it as the pendant;
It abandons true awareness of self and other.
The family is all the elements;
The yoginī is the one who has the family.
In the perfect union of the two
The pendant should fully enter the belly.
Up to the taste of all the elements,
It becomes cut off.
By accomplishing the elixir
There is increase for all people.
They abide nowhere at all;
They come close to Khecara itself.
The buddhas and the minds of awakening
Dwell without defilement in the center.
In this way all the treasuries of qualities
Abide in the state of right speech.
Holding the great mantra that is beyond expression,
One proclaims it to all people.
One should know it as indestructible.
The earth is Vajradhārī herself.
All the substances of the elements, without exception,
Abide perfectly as heroes by name.
The elements of the skull fragment
I shall explain each on its own.
Nails and teeth flowing,
Each divided into eight;
The four elements, earth and the rest,
And likewise the four substances.
Thus in sites of three each
One should join all the heroes.
Hair and body hair flowing,
Skin and dirt flowing,
Flesh and loose flesh flowing,
Sinews and channels flowing,
Bones and hide flowing,
Kidneys and strength flowing,
Heart and the net flowing,
Eyes and lymph flowing,
Bile and wind flowing,
Lungs and channels flowing;
The substances flowing there
Are the nape and the liver flowing,
Stomach and breath flowing,
Feces and urine flowing,
The edges of the junctures and dirt flowing,
Intestinal lining and spittle flowing,
Pus and lymph flowing,
Blood and the menstrual flower flowing,
Dust and vapor flowing,
Fat and belly fat flowing,
Tears and semen flowing,
Phlegm and the absence of fever flowing,
Snot and marrow flowing:
These are the characteristics of the heroes' elements.
In this way, segment by segment,
The elements enter into one another.
The nature of each one, too:
Through the wind of exhaustion, Kapālin;
To dissolve within all bliss,
Mahākaṅkāla goes.
Because of mind and intention,
Kaṅkāla is everywhere emptiness.
Through Vikaṭadaṃṣṭrin
All is nondual, innate and clear.
Surāri is the great form,
Consciousness, the deity and the one who has the deity.
Amitābha is beyond words;
Vajraprabha has the nature of vajra light rays.
The body whose nature is vajra
Is to be known there as Vajradeha.
Aṅkurika, by merely being seen,
Brings delight to all things.
To bind consciousness into matter,
Vajrajaṭila goes.
Making the three worlds into the supreme food
Is done through the progress of Mahāvīra.
To dwell together with the ḍākinīs,
Vajrahūṃkāra goes.
Subhadra, without attainment,
Has abandoned the world and the worldly.
Self-arisen in all beings,
He is known as Vajrabhadra.
Through the state of Mahābhairava
The imprints of karma are smeared with ash.
Though Virūpākṣa does not look,
He sees the characteristics of all.
Mahābala is all the mantras,
The embodiment of the three aspects.
Ratnavajra is great in silence;
The undefiled elements are everywhere.
Hayagrīva, upon the great earth,
Is to be applied to all that has life.
He is to fill all the life breaths;
Therefore he is called Ākāśagarbha.
Glorious Heruka is always empty,
Having the nature of the four bodies.
Padmanarteśvara, form
And formless, goes into yoga.
Because he illuminates through great bliss,
He is called Vairocana.
Vajrasattva, made to stand proudly,
Is declared to be supreme bliss.
The union of all the heroes
Is within all the elements.
In an instant, a moment, a brief while,
One should know the characteristic of tasting.
The exhaustion of all these elements
Is supremely and greatly wondrous.
The numbers of the natural elements
Are the hours, the nāgas and the arrows.
They are widely known as yoginīs,
Come down through the succession of the lineage.
When the elements too are analyzed,
None of them arises by itself.
It is not consciousness, it is not wisdom;
All is empty, it does not exist.
Through not knowing the gold-making elixir,
One does not know supreme bliss.
That which accomplishes its purpose by itself
Is the characteristic of the deity of the touchstone family.
Therefore one should meditate on the yoginī
Who is called Great Diligence.
Sealing all the elements,
She arises perfectly as the seat of yoga.
Whatever is all, everywhere,
That is seen as the Tathāgata.
In the Ḍāka and the Saṃvara tantras,
In the Later Abhidhāna,
And in the tantras of the Arising of All Conduct,
It is fully taught in terms of entities.
Therefore this great tantra
Was spoken by the Sage, the supreme person.
The great rites that abide
In segment after segment are to be drawn out there.
The minor acts that are named
Belong to the sites of the elements.
One should take up the elements in their order,
Up to the domain of complete gathering;
And with the first verse and the rest,
Up to the domain of completion.
This tantra is chiefly yoga;
Its rites are not extensive.
Or else all yoga
Is to be held as a collection of rites.
One's own reality is always the rite,
Whether it is done or not done.
The characteristics of the application of the yogas
Are perfectly explained as the natural rite.
In this way, in all the tantras,
The mantrin should know it supremely.
Or else, whatever yoga it resembles,
The elements abide in resemblance to it.
The substances of the elixir,
Compounded into the twenty-four great medicines
By the heroes themselves,
Are taught as the progress toward liberation.
When one delights in the supreme secret,
The self of all always dwells.
Those sixteen are the great substances,
Given in eight pairs of half verses.

Truly arisen as sound and form, Hard to find in the three worlds, The full union of the churner and the churned, The unsurpassed form of the central one: The pill should always be worshiped. The messenger (dūtī), whose very self is the coemergent, With the mind gone deep within, The buddha drops of one's own fluid, Together with honey, blood, and camphor, Having fully entered the midst of the gathering, With ring finger and thumb, into the mouth, Taste the drink of the moon. With these, which have become the five nectars, Perform likewise the four offerings. Gods and humans, through bliss, The sixteen portions are precious. Thus, by all these verses, The five elements are explained. By the syllable ra, the element of blood; The syllable hā is held to be the mountain-born; The syllable sya is the fragrant leaf; The syllable pa is held to be bark; The syllable ra is declared to be ghandha, sulfur; The syllable me should be recalled as flower; The syllable ra likewise is rābiṇa; Again, the syllable me is flesh; The syllable sa is born from frankincense; The syllable rba is held to be grass; The syllable tma is declared to be the hollow-born; The syllable na is fragrant oil; The syllable sa is the powder of teeth; The syllable dā is the ocean-born; The syllable sthi likewise is hoarfrost; The syllable ta is the camphor of the essence. Fully arisen as sound and form: This is in the space between blood, semen, and the jewel. Hard to find in the three worlds: This is self-awareness reaching the lotus. Through the unions of the churner and the churned: This is the mingling by the self-arisen. The breath of the supreme central one, too: This is holding it for an instant in the central channel. The pill that should always be worshiped: This is the nectar, the secret water. The messenger whose very self is the coemergent: This is play through the union of the two organs. The mind gone deep within: This is the melting of the elements as well. The buddhas, the drops of one's own fluid: These arise from the union of what melts. Honey, blood, and camphor together: These arise from a sixteen-year-old Kāminī. Those who fully dwell in the midst of the gathering: They apply all this to the coemergent. Placing it in the mouth with ring finger and thumb: This is the blood that arises from union in absorption. Tasting the drink of peace: This is whatever comes, entire, at the time of emission. These that become the five nectars: This is the human oil arising from emission. Performing likewise the four offerings: This is the four in equal parts, together with camphor. The bliss of gods and humans: This is what arises from all the joys. The sixteen precious portions: All of them have the nature of bliss. Thus all the heroes Should apply these as the substances of the elements. The elixir arises from that. The chief, by the fire and wind of wisdom, Having brought about exhaustion and killing, Then likewise transference and descent: Exhaustion is the trickling of awakening; Through killing, too, there comes to be no transference; Transference is throughout all the channels; Descent is into the lotus of the navel. By binding these, One should make it descend into all the elements. Killing is the destruction of entities; Increase is in the world-realms. Knowing there the rite of the fire offering, Attain the state of a universal monarch. The nectar should be burned in the hearth. Mañjuvajra, in direct perception, The one endowed with yoga will come to see. It carries one across all the grounds. The life of which the great one possesses the Dharma: The Dharma will come to pervade everywhere. Its very nature is Mahāvīryā, Supreme as the self of the thirty-seven, Perfected by her through the mantra of the maṇḍala, Supreme self of wisdom and skillful means. The great goddess, white in color, She who is born of omniscient wisdom: All sentient beings come under one's power, And one attains the state of a universal monarch. oṃ ga i ra ndra sa jā ta la bā sa da rba pra sa bā tvā da tma ma ne hā hūṃ ma hūṃ te phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ svā phaṭ hā phaṭ svā hā oṃ ma hā bi ryā yai hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā Thus is the very self of the reality of mantra, Invoked by all the deities. Then, for the wheel-shaped diagram of the yantra, Forty-nine squares, fifty less one, Having been drawn properly with saffron, This garland of secret mantra should be set out. The Sumbha mantra, in reversed order, Should be set out in its proper sequence. Then, having made the figure of a human, One should again add a circle. On it the root mantra is written, And the name of the target placed inside. The central segment is the target, Placed inside its mantra. The mantrin writes on birch bark and the like, Marking it outwardly and secretly. Applying such a yantra well, One should meditate on the three grounds. From the union of all the heroes, Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss, Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata: The Blessed Lord spoke this.

This is the forty-ninth chapter, called "The Rite of the Root Mantra of Heruka, the Glorious True Meditative Absorption, the Characteristics of the Rite of the Full Application of Mahāvīryā, the Secret Elixir and So Forth, and the Rite of Increase," from the great king of tantras, the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the heroes, Vajrapāṇi and the others,
All the mantrins, rising up,
With praise, offering, and prostration,
Roused Vārāhī fully:
"O goddess, my mother,
Fully worshiped by all the goddesses,
You are the mother of all of these;
Heart of wisdom, I pay homage and bow.
Out of compassion for all sentient beings
You emanate yourself in bodily form;
It is fitting that you show me, in every way,
The great kindness of your compassion.
I ask the Blessed Lord,
Who dwells fully in great bliss:
Of the hosts of rites of all the tantras
I do not know the means of accomplishment.
If, in tantra after tantra,
All the rites have been taught,
Why, then, is this taught here
For those who have attained pure wisdom?
For all people to enter
This very tantra is not fitting.
To explain it again is faulty.
I do not understand; I have doubts."
Vārāhī said nothing at all;
Entering the mouth of the Blessed One,
She became his wisdom,
And he gave the reply.
The Blessed One, the self of all phenomena,
Having set his mind in meditative absorption,
Uttered speech
Through all the families of Vārāhī:
"O, O, all you heroes,
In whom wisdom and the knowable are mingled,
Whatever wisdom you see
Dissolves into this and that.
Yet it operates separately
Because of the various dispositions of sentient beings.

In the great tantra of nonduality itself,
In Vajraḍāka and Saṃvara,
And in the other yoga tantras as well,
The Samāja and the rest, of great meaning,
By these the various meanings
Of this tantra are expressed.
All of them, possessing the host of rites,
Make it fully clear.
In the tantra, as the expressed and the expresser,
Through it one enters into the subtle.
Each has its own characteristic nature,
Endowed with the segments of the skull and so forth.
There the phenomenon of consciousness
Is explained as the segment of the skull.
The karma of all sentient beings
And their afflictions should be known with ease.
Otherwise, in the tantra of the Omniscient,
The letters would not arise.
Within the span of a single year
The twelve months should be known,
Turned by the wheel of the six seasons.
Sentient beings are supremely like a dream;
Imbued with the stain of ignorance,
They do not know bliss.
By the sequence of the hāradola,
Consciousness, with vigilance,
Is twenty thousand, and a thousand,
And six hundred more besides.
Sixty periods of the breath
Are well known as a single day.
Through the breaths of the hāradola,
By alternation, it is marked by the dola.
The breath of the two, for the wise,
Going out, coming in, comes to dissolve.
The yogin comes to contemplate it,
Yet does not draw near to omniscience.
Abiding in the yoga of the subtle and the subtler,
He practices everything fully.
Its numerals, outside,
Should be set as twelve upon the ground.
Just as the divisions of the sun are explained,
So too are the twelve limbs.
The rising of the lunar mansions and the planets
Takes place at all times.
The element of each, as well,
Comes to rise at its own time.
Death at its time, and likewise the rest,
Will be explained by this sequence.
For one who knows the application of dāttyā and the rest,
This application is to the mother letters.
Beginning with the month of Puṣya and so on,
In the sun's northern course,
The twelve months, in that way,
Should be set out in their order, one after another.
Beginning from the directions, east and the rest,
The wise one counts from the morning.
The third is likewise the thirteenth,
The fourth again the fourteenth,
The fifth as the fifteenth.
I shall explain it; listen now.
These, being equal and endowed with equality,
The practitioner should bring into accord.
Here there are ignorance and formations,
Consciousness, and name and form,
The six sense sources and contact,
Feeling, and likewise craving,
Grasping and existence,
Birth, and aging and death.
Likewise, with rohita and the rest,
The signs (rāsi), beginning with Makara,
Should be held to be twelve.
First, ignorance is Makara
And rohita, and likewise the rest.
Second, mohita and the Vase
Clearly go to formations.
Third, the Auspicious One and the Fish
Should be known as consciousness.
Fourth, the Bull and the Ram
Should be held to be name and form.
Fifth is the Tortoise
And briṣa: the six sense sources suffice.
Sixth, Makara and the Pair
Always go to contact.
Seventh is raṇḍaka,
And likewise feeling, the supreme.
Eighth, mikira and the Lion,
Supremely concordant, are craving.
Ninth, bidri and the Maiden
Are held to be grasping.
Tenth, distraction of mind and the Scales
Are declared to be existence.
Eleventh, the dream
And brika, and likewise birth.
Twelfth, the Lord of the Earth,
The Bow, and aging and dying.
Thus the Lord of the Earth and the rest,
The dream and so forth, and likewise the others,
Distraction and the rest, bhidrika and the rest,
Likewise mikira and the rest,
raṇḍa and the rest, Makara and the rest,
The Tortoise and the rest, the Bull and the rest,
The Auspicious One and the rest, and delusion and the rest:
Therefore these are the twelve months.
Whoever is born on these days
Is held to bear that characteristic.
In an instant, a moment, a brief while,
It should be marked on the measuring rod.
Even what passes in opening and closing the eyes
Comes from an extremely subtle time.
If the mind abides in meditative absorption,
One goes by stages to the twelfth ground.
Or else, if absorption does not abide,
One is held to go to the middling destiny.
The inferior, all such people,
Go to the hells and the like.
The pleasure and suffering of those who are led
Are fully experienced through karma.
Through that whose time is nondual,
All are born on the ground of intrinsic nature.
Even so, those born there
Eat the fruit through their own karma.
Thus, merely by apprehending it fully,
One is endowed with the yoga of the skull segments.
By the reasoning explained before,
It descends into the center of the elements.
At the time when wind is active,
The yogin will be born.
In nine days, months,
And years, the wise should know the death.
Then he will undertake kingship;
That it is so is beyond doubt.
One born on the day of rohita
Will die in the Lord of the Earth.
One born at the time when earth is active,
If he does not die in eight days, months,
Or in one year,
Is without doubt a bodhisattva.
At the time when dream is active,
One is brave and bold, and overcomes others;
Merit and evil are mingled,
And one dies at fifty-four.
In distraction of mind, one is killed by an enemy.
One born on its day,
If at sixty-four years and ten months
He has not died,
He who had little wealth will afterward
Come to be prosperous.
On the day of bhitri one dies.
One born on the day of that time,
If he does not die in the fifth, the second,
The third, or the first year,
For him all desired aims are diminished;
He kills father and mother,
And is mixed with austerity.
In mikira one dies in one's own house.
Whoever is born here on that day,
If he does not die
In the second, fifth, third, or ninth year,
Is equal to the Omniscient One.
In the Widow one dies in water.
Whoever is born on the day of that time,
If he does not die in the second or third year,
Is intelligent,
A doer of evil, afflicted with illness;
The men and women are fortunate.
In Makara one dies. One born in that very one,
If in fifteen, three, and five
Days, months, and years
He does not die, then in short
He is complete in every good quality,
And becomes a king who guards the borders.
In the Tortoise and the Pair one dies.
One born on the day of that time
Dies in the second, eighth, or ninth year.
If he does not die then,
He should be known as an ordinary being,
Going to low and to high estate.

In the Bull one dies in one's own house.
One born on its day,
If, says the wise one, he does not die
In the second, eighth, or ninth year,
Is perfect in every kind of lordship,
Yet given to anger and the like, and to wrong views.
In the Auspicious One one dies by being killed.
One born on that very day,
If in the fifth, ninth, or tenth year
This person does not die,
Is poor yet perfect in Dharma,
Adorned by everything.
In delusion one passes away in water.
One born on its day,
If in the first month of the second or ninth year
He does not die at that time,
Is lowly in everything, bound for hell,
Of evil thoughts, his nature sinful;
Killed by thieves and the like,
He dies on the day of rohita.
Thus it should be known in summary,
If meditative absorption has not been attained.
The actions that make births manifest
Are obtained otherwise.
Whoever is close to that very thing
Should guard it well;
On the day in the sequence explained,
There is no doubt of release in this.
The segment of the skull itself:
These should be meditated on as bringing good fortune.
Whatever channel travels to the ḍākas,
In its center one practices oneself.
That should be known as the egg-born family.
It should be guarded by stirring the scattering.
The yogin will come to hold the body.
Here the little toe should be moved.
The soles of the feet and the gate of the crown,
All the junctures should be guarded.
Having made the vowels and consonants equal,
One should dissolve them into the elements.
Consciousness is unmoving;
Make everything its nature.
From the center of the head, without fail,
Facing downward, it comes to descend and go.
Perfect in its central region,
The yogin goes to the end of existence.
In the count of the time of the a ba dhū tī,
I shall explain it clearly, just as it is.
A fortnight and three years should be known:
This is death at the time of people.
Whatever the measure of a hundred years,
That should be examined in the center.
Of the two, the rasanā and the lalanā,
The month of birth should be marked.
Month by month it comes to be destroyed;
It has twenty-four parts.
The count of a single breath as well,
Therefore, from two years it becomes twofold;
By threefold and so forth, all of them,
And then twenty-four-fold.
It should be known as sky, hollow, and eye;
Afterward it goes into periods of the day.
Up to the twelfth year
There are periods of five by five.
From the sixtieth year the days increase;
Thirty-seven, and three fortnights.
Those that increase by five by five
Should be made the numerals of the periods of the day.
The death of all people
Is known in three fortnights and in years.
Through the years of twenty-four,
Because humans go endowed with discernment.
Having entered the house of earth,
One ransoms death by the mind.
Contemplate the segment of the skull
Where glorious Heruka has taken his abode.
By the yoga of the sequence of counting,
The periods of day and night should be marked.
Marked according to the time,
It is done step by step.
At the time of the northern course,
The sequence is the goddess Puṣya and the rest.
Ten multiplied by two, and four:
For the wise, the periods of the day.
What is to be known at the time of the equinox
Will be explained in its own junction of time.
The three tens and the six
Begin the periods of the night.
In a hundred, by the distinctions of time,
It should be known from its center.
Ten multiplied by two, and six:
Make these the numerals of the periods of the days.
Ten multiplied by three, and four:
Examine these as the periods of the night.
Ten multiplied by two, and eight:
So the periods of the days.
Ten multiplied by three, and just two:
Examine these as the periods of the night.
At the equinox, joining two in the center,
Examine it fully as just thirty.
By the two sets of thirty
The periods of each day are divided.
Three tens, and just two:
Examine these as the day of the days.
Two tens and eight:
Make these the numerals of the night.
For the numeral of three tens
It is fully joined with four.
Ten multiplied by two, and six:
Make these the numerals of the periods of the night.
Ten multiplied by three, and six:
The days, the day of those who have wisdom.
Ten multiplied by two, and four:
These are known as the periods of the dusty ones.
The southern course is well explained;
It is known by the time of the months.
Ten multiplied by three, and four:
These are fully explained as the periods of the daytime.
Ten multiplied by two, and six:
So too should the night be marked.
Ten multiplied by three, and just two:
So too the periods of the days.
Ten multiplied by two, and six:
These are known as the periods of the night.
Marked by ten multiplied by three,
So too the periods of the equinox and so forth.
Ten multiplied by three, for the nights;
In the center of the bi shu, two likewise.
Ten multiplied by two, and eight:
Consider these the daytime of the days.
Ten threefold, and just two:
These are examined as the periods of the dusty ones.
Ten twofold, and six:
The periods of the days, in sequence.
Ten threefold, and four:
So too the periods of the dusty ones.
Three hundred, and likewise sixty:
The breaths that fill the periods.
The number of the periods, sixty,
Is declared to be a single day.
Just as the months are in their sequence,
So they are marked by the sequences.
The periods, breaths and so forth, all complete,
Increase and decrease in sequence.
By the distinction of rite and non-rite,
The breaths are fully grasped in sequence.
Thus sky, sky, and taste, likewise
Moon and eye, and likewise the periods,
Should be known in a single year,
Marked by the days and the periods.
By doing so, the yogin
Will ransom death from time.
In the center of the navel is a great lotus,
Just as Vajravārāhī is.
On the lotus that reaches all the junctures,
One should let it trickle like hoarfrost.
From the center of the navel to the twelve ends,
It is what goes and comes.
The drop of life, as it travels,
Descends into all the junctures.
The wise one, by the practice of a single year,
Should dissolve it into that very thing.
Like the form of a burning
Lamp of sesame oil, facing downward,
Like the flower of the plantain,
The wise one holds it as if hanging down.
At the navel is the form shaped like a,
Short, long, and the self of the prolonged:
The form of the coemergent mudrā,
Where vowels and mother letters abide as nondual.
Meditation and the practitioner are explained
As dull, middling, and sharp,
Especially sharp, and exceedingly sharp:
All are examined in terms of these.

Because one accomplishes it by oneself,
Therefore it should be accomplished by others too.
With power and might in this very world,
One inspires confidence in all people,
And afterward they become disciples.
With the mind moistened by the aims of self and others,
Having obtained the twelve vowels,
One should explain virtue and nonvirtue.
In the twelve wheels
The lord of the vowels should be marked.
By the syllable ā the wise one obtains wisdom
And every aim of the Dharma.
By the syllable ā, oppression,
Aging and death are definitively declared;
The fears of existence, without exception,
Will be seen, and one falls from the path.
By fully obtaining the syllable ī,
One goes to lordship over the earth.
If the syllable ī is truly obtained,
One will die swiftly.
By obtaining the syllable ū,
One comes to remember one's births.
If the syllable ū is obtained,
One becomes attached to sense objects.
If the syllable i is truly obtained,
One attains the state of a bodhisattva.
With the syllable ai the mind is bewildered,
And the six destinies and the rest become a terror.
The syllable o is of the perfections;
By it one attains the grounds there, it shows.
By obtaining the numeral au,
One comes to break the sacred commitments.
The syllable aṃ: by seeing the bodhisattvas,
One is liberated in an instant.
By truly obtaining the syllable aḥ,
One doubts the aggregates and elements.
These and the rest, without exception,
Even if not spoken of, should be accomplished.
The Guru is the treatise itself;
Confidence should be placed in the threefold Dharma.

This is called the opening words, named the means of accomplishment of the characteristics of the yoga of the one with the segment of the skull, and the sādhana of the wheel of the maṇḍala. This is the first section.

Then I shall explain
The characteristics of Mahākaṅkāla.
By oneself, through the yoga like a donkey,
The force of the mind should be kept from entering.
The mountain of the equal elements
Is meditated on at the place of the crown.
The lotus-fiber thread, facing downward,
She who is lightning, going everywhere:
In its center the great heroine,
Having the shape of a donkey, should be meditated on.
All the maṇḍala is perfectly complete;
A great offering should be imagined.
In its center the great hero,
Through the union of Mahākaṅkāla.
By the threads, in the center of the navel,
Are the very unions of the churner and the churned.
The form shaped like the syllable hā,
A single thread from the head:
With a mind like a mirror,
One should rest in the center of the skull.
By the streams of nectar from the vase,
It comes to trickle into the center of the aperture.
By states such as dissolution and enjoyment,
The course of the mind should be shown.
In that aperture, by the yoga of the donkey,
One will see even seven births.
The three shapes that arise from the mother,
And likewise the three that arise from the father,
All the elements, and those seven,
Will be seen by meditating on the yoga.
From all the elements, whose nature is bliss,
Mahākaṅkāla truly arose.
By meditating on lā ma and the rest,
One will see the forms that dwell there.
In the yoga of the donkey's shape,
With a face of blood, bearing a trident,
One also knows when life is spent,
And will also see long life.
Earlier, the elephant and the rest:
The hero sees that by himself.
In all the great channels
The life of Kaṅkāla should be made to circulate fully.
When it moves, the donkey should be known;
One will see its various natures.
Foods such as sā lu rice:
All things are seen as resembling them,
Yet they do not exist there;
All the elements, too, are delusion.
Just as in a person, through the times,
It is to be known by the element of the elephant,
So by the great Kaṅkālas,
Through the conditions of the elements such as sā li rice,
By apprehending the worldly nature,
One sees that form of oneself.
Those families of many kinds
Should be known by those who hold to yoga.
On the separate days of ignorance and the rest,
By them they are known as illnesses.
By mere illnesses such as fever,
The great illnesses should be indicated.
There, the illness of the day of ignorance:
The good should guard against it.
At the fifteenth transference,
One dies, or the karma is virtuous or nonvirtuous.
Through one whose self is supremely the fire of wisdom,
One will be liberated in five nights.
At the time of aging and death,
One dies at the third transference;
Or else one will see the habitual tendencies
Of virtuous and nonvirtuous karma.
By grasping the bliss of self-awareness,
One will be liberated in seven nights.
The death on the day of birth
Is likewise at the third transference;
Karma goes to virtue or nonvirtue;
One will be liberated in seven nights.
At the three transferences of existence
One will die; in five nights
They will be liberated.
Or else one is not liberated;
In the times of the fourth that has passed,
Realize all of that bliss.
In those families that change in that way,
It should be known in its proper sequence.
The transference of death of grasping
Is at the third and at the fourth.
If the karma is virtuous or nonvirtuous,
One will be liberated in ten nights.
One who has every abundance of merit
And is endowed with intelligence accomplishes non-transference.
At the three transferences of craving one dies;
The karma is virtuous and nonvirtuous;
One should look to what has entered within the jewel.
In seven nights one is surely liberated;
There is no doubt about my words.
At the five transferences of feeling
One will die, and the karma is likewise;
One is liberated in nine or ten,
Through entering the center of the vajra and lotus.
The transference on the day of contact:
It should be known that one dies at the eighth.
Thus karma is virtuous or nonvirtuous;
It should be dissolved through the six limbs.
On the day of twenty less one,
One is liberated, or else one is not liberated.
The death of the six sense sources
Should be looked at in terms of virtuous and nonvirtuous karma;
The eight transferences should be known.
One will see the illnesses of the elements;
In ten nights one will be liberated;
It should be dissolved into the four wheels.
The karma of death in name and form:
The transferences of the third
Or of the fifth should be known;
In eight nights there is release.

The rite should be performed by which the mind
Comes to rest, having reached the place of the drop.
The karma of death of consciousness:
Again, at the transference of the eighth and the tenth,
It should be known on the ground.
Or else there is release in that very thing:
Having the form of sixteen halves of halves,
It should be seized by the wind of wisdom.
The karma of death of formations:
Likewise, of the third and the seventh,
Because it has been looked upon, it is transference;
By that view one will be liberated.
Having come to know the two drops,
One should dissolve them by going upward.
By this one becomes free of illness and death.
Therefore, the buddha Vajradhara,
The virtuous one whose self is the thirty-seven:
Again, the maṇḍala should be meditated on.
By the yogin of the chief, Mahākaṅkāla,
Everything is seen.

This is the second section, endowed with the reality of the instruction for one who meditates on Mahākaṅkāla in the yoga of the donkey.

Then, through the union of Kaṅkāla,
Born in their own birthplaces, land by land,
The yoginīs and the chief heroines
Should be known by one endowed with wisdom.
At Aṭṭahāsa is the goddess of dust,
The yoginī who is chief of all.
In her place dwells the goddess of the place.
At the ka dam tree is Mahāghaṇṭā,
The constant one of that goddess.
The kṣetrapāla is Great Face.
She is the illusion of the bliss of Kaṅkāla;
From the great self, renunciation comes about.
These are sealed by Kaṅkāla.
From the aperture of Oḍḍiyāna they reach upward;
Rising from one's own element, consciousness
Goes by stages into all the lands:
The mountain of Kolla and Vāruṇya,
Devīkoṭa and Birjakaṃ,
Eruṇya, and then the town,
The town of Aila, and Kashmir,
The town of Maru, and likewise
The field of Puṇḍavadhana,
Jayantī and the town of Pṛṣṭa,
Saupare, and likewise Caritra,
Oḍḍiyāna and Jālandhara,
Kṣereke and the town of Māyā,
Aṃbuke and Rājagṛha,
The land of Tibet, and likewise
Mālava, Pulira and the rest:
These are the twenty-four places.
The mountain peak and the tā la tree,
The pa ṭa, the ā mra, and likewise the golden tree,
The matted-locks tree and the overpowering one,
They truly abide on the mountain summit.
The great plain and the grassy hollow,
The a śvad tha and the many-storied house,
The u dum bā ra and the śal ma li,
The ka rañja, and the aśoka they abide in,
The golden tree and the sā la,
The cu ta, the dhā tu, the dil la ki,
The heaped rock and the honey tree:
Formerly they truly abode there.
The birthplace of dust, the body of the scattered,
Mahābalā and the place of yoga,
The skull, karma, and the dust exceedingly fierce,
The birthplace, the heroes, the body of the scattered,
The Mahābalās, and likewise blood;
The birthplace of heroes arises from power;
The birthplace arising from the moon,
That arising from the family of Mahābalā,
Bhairava arising from blood, likewise,
Born from fat and from the great battlefront,
The birthplace of flowers, and likewise the two:
Again, that very word should be recalled.
These goddesses arising from the families
Are maidens of coemergent form.
Mahāśrī and Agnimukhā,
And the kṣetrapāla with the Supreme Face,
Known as Agnimukha,
The knower of the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Holding its transference,
They emanate the two yoginīs.
Through his union with what she desires,
The heroes rely truly on nonduality.
For the sake of benefiting sentient beings,
Dwelling in the vajra maṇḍala,
With moving forms that act everywhere,
They go into all the lands.
Laṅkeśvarī and Ūrdhvakeśī,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Fang;
The one explained as Ūrdhvakeśa
Has the bliss of Kaṅkāla as cause.
The particulars of the supreme yoginīs
And the rest should be known as taught before.
Twisted Ear and the Fierce One,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Body;
Then, or else, the One with Cause
And the supreme sovereign lady, the Great Fierce One:
Through the emptiness of the bliss of Kaṅkāla
It should be known as the supreme state.
Peaceful Form and Fire,
And the kṣetrapāla Aṭṭahāsa;
Fire and Great Delusion,
And Great Sound also, should be known.
The Caṇḍī of Kaṅkāla:
Great bliss in the center of the blaze.
Agnimukhā and Mahāvīryā,
And the kṣetrapāla Sound of the Bell;
She who is known as Mahāvīryā
Is the source of the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Tawny Renown and Fearsome Eyes,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Shanks;
Bhairavī, or else Locanā,
Is the one who fills the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Great Terror and Mahābalā,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Elephant;
Or else Elephant Ear,
And the kṣetrapāla Elephant-Eared,
Riding upon the bliss of Kaṅkāla,
Its nature present in all the channels.
Cow Ear and the One of the Mountain Peak,
And the kṣetrapāla Mahābhairava;
Then again, the shanks of the hollow,
Dissolving into the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
The Traveler and Fearsome Face,
And the kṣetrapāla Fang;
The Deluder, Great Face,
Attached to the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Vetālī and the Fair-Faced One,
And the kṣetrapāla with Hairy Shanks;
Or else Great Upturned Fang,
Who devours the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Cāmuṇḍā and Mahādevī,
And the kṣetrapāla with testicles like pots;
Or else the Great Goddess,
And the kṣetrapāla with the potlike paunch;
Or else he is Mahādeva,
Who dances in the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
The Most Reverent One and Trijaṭā,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Fang;
Or else he should be known as Trijaṭa,
Gone beyond in the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Lightning Face and Bell Sound,
And the kṣetrapāla Owl-Faced;
Renowned indeed as Sound of the Bell,
Here he enjoys the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Fire Face and Mahādevī,
And the kṣetrapāla Terrifier;
Or else he should be known as Mahādeva,
Radiant with the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Likewise she who dwells at the ka rañja,
Known also as Mahāghaṇṭā,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Strength,
Beautified by the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Secret Renown and Mahādevī,
Fierce Form and Mahābalā,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Sound,
And Fierce Form and Great Strength,
Arisen wholly from the bliss of Kaṅkāla,
Endowed with supreme, unchanging yoga.
Caṇḍī and the Fierce One,
And the kṣetrapāla known as Blazing;
Then, or else, Fierce Form,
Garbed in the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Mother of the World and Upturned Hair,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Meru;
Then, or else, Ūrdhvakeśa,
The one who meditates on the bliss of Kaṅkāla.

The Terrifying One and Mahābalā,
And likewise the kṣetrapāla Terror;
Mahābala and Fierce Form,
Who has the essence of the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Pūtanā and Mahādevī,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Observance;
Through the power of the bliss of Kaṅkāla,
Therefore all the senses are bliss.
Vipanasamā and Bell Sound,
And the kṣetrapāla Great Ears;
Or else Bell Ringer,
The trickling of the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Coemergent Renown and Wealth Renown,
And likewise the supreme Mahāvīra,
And the kṣetrapāla Most Invincible,
The one who gauges the bliss of Kaṅkāla.
Equal to Empowerment and Born Sovereign,
And the kṣetrapāla Lord of Variety;
By abiding in the bliss of Kaṅkāla,
One becomes oneself the bliss of desire.

A vajra, a head, and a trident,
A silk banner, a vajra, and a short spear,
A pestle, and likewise a noose,
A vajra chain and a hook,
A noose, a plough, and a khaṭvāṅga,
One holding a vajra chain in hand,
A staff, a short spear, and a curved knife,
A short spear, and one with a vajra chain,
A curved knife, and likewise a sword,
A vajra, and a short spear, a plough,
A club, a vajra, and likewise a hook,
A makara banner, and a hammer.
They are to be known as having peaceful faces.

The Snarling One and the Most Terrifying,
She Who Grants Mastery of the Dharma,
The Sovereign of Yoga, Supreme of All,
She Who Grants Mastery of Desire,
She Who Grants the Field of Utter Delusion,
The Snarling One, Lady of the Field of Delusion,
The Greatly Terrifying, and likewise another,
Fierce Face and Crusher of Foes,
The Bhairavī of the Field of Delusion,
She Who Grants the Supreme to All Heroes,
Freed Enjoyment, Destroyer of Evil,
She Who Grants the Supreme of All Bliss,
She Who Fulfills All Wishes,
The Snarling, Wrathful, Terrifying One,
She of the Seat of Flesh, and another,
She Who Grants the Supreme Mastery of Bliss,
The Beautiful One, Needed by All,
She Who Grants Worldly Accomplishments,
The Snarling Goddess Who Terrifies,
The Most Wrathful, Beautiful of Form,
She Who Grants All Mastery,
She Who Grants All Accomplishments,
The Terrifying One, the Accomplisher, She Who Grants Renown,
Black Form and the Great Wrathful One,
She Who Rides the Terror of All Foes,
She Who Subjugates All,
She Who Terrifies All Sentient Beings,
She Who Is Victorious over All Wheels,
The Terrifier, and likewise another,
She of Peaceful Face, Beautiful of Form,
She Who Favors the Practitioners,
The Renowned One and the Highly Praised:
Thus are they all, each in order.

At Aṭṭahāsa she sits upon a wheel;
At Kolagiri she sits upon a corpse;
At Vāruṇya, at Siddhasiṃpaḍi;
At Devīkoṭa she dwells upon a preta;
At Viraja she sits upon a plank;
At Eranya she sits upon a pig;
Then in the town she sits upon a preta;
In the town of Ele she dwells upon a donkey;
In Kashmir she dwells upon a yakṣa;
In the land of Maru she dwells upon a buffalo;
In the town, upon the back of a head;
At Puṇḍravardhana she dwells upon a corpse;
At Jayantī she dwells upon a skeleton;
In the town of Priṣṭi she dwells upon a heap;
At Sopāra she dwells upon a palm;
In Caritra she dwells at Karañja;
In Oḍḍiyāna she sits upon a māra;
At Jālandhara she dwells upon a corpse;
At Kṣirika she dwells upon fire;
In the town of Māyā, upon coarse cloth;
At Aṃbuke she dwells upon a lion;
At Rājagṛha she dwells upon a wheel;
In the land of Tibet she dwells upon a preta;
And likewise at Mālava, dwelling on a corpse.
Through the thorough application of Kaṅkāla,
In such fields accomplishment comes about.

The bliss that arises from Kaṅkāla
Rises up fully from the root of the channels.
Pervading all the junctures,
That fluid of wisdom will be seen.
This speech, arisen from the Headless One,
Is the very eating of that element.
From the space where the two organs abide,
Through that, bliss will be seen.
All the heroes and yoginīs,
And the wise, become as nothing at all.
Its nature, in beings as illusion,
Will be seen as the drawing in of the belly.
Through hanging down, in every place
That nectar goes and comes.
As it trickles, the yoginīs
Emanate their own field-born ones.
To those outer, inner, and secret in nature,
One should make a great offering.
By cutting through the mountain of ignorance,
All accomplishments will be achieved.

Then, in the center of the maṇḍala explained before,
One should meditate on Kaṅkāla,
And also on the hero, lord of beings,
Whose nature is wisdom and skillful means.
In the places that were stated, the yoginīs,
Then those named Most Fierce and so on.
As a cause of accomplishment, the practitioner
Should also hold a gathering there.
Food, drink, and the like should be properly prepared,
Offerings such as the five enjoyments,
And the vessels of torma to be enjoyed,
Fresh meat, fish, and so on,
All the foods and substances,
The five nectars, and likewise others.
Having set the vessels on the left,
One should display the wrathful one on the right.
At a solitary tree or a charnel ground,
On a mountain, in a hermitage, or in a house,
In villages and on the outskirts of towns,
In an empty house, at a crossroads,
Those who move in water and those who roam the plains,
The ground, the houses of the mātṛkās, and the shores:

Kālī, Raudrī, and Mahāraudrī,
God-Given and Perfect Support,
Snarling Kālī and Bhairava,
Beyond Joy and the vināyaka,
Cāmuṇḍī and Wrathful Form,
The goddess Madhyamā and the Mother,
Jayā and Vijayā,
Ajitā and Aparājitā,
Bhadrakālī and Mahākālī,
Stout Kālī and the Yoginī,
Aindrī, Cāndrī, and Raudrī,
The Hostile One, the Pendant One, Mistress of the Thirty,
Kampo, Jamme, and the Suckling One,
The yoginī who dwells in the village,
Wrathful Form and Great Form,
The Fanged One, the Fierce One, the Snarling One,
The goddess with a garland of skulls,
She who holds the khaṭvāṅga,
The Dwarf and she with an axe in hand,
She with a vajra in hand, and likewise the bow:
These five ḍākinīs, great indeed,
Accomplish all actions.
The Queen of the Maṇḍala of Union,
And likewise the chief, Vajreśvarī,
The great body of the Tathāgata,
Stainless yoga, emptiness:
By the command of Vajreśvara,
They are invited here from every side.

oṃ kakka kaḍḍhana babba bandhana khakhkha khādana sarbaduṣṭāna hana hana ghātaya ghātaya accomplish the purpose of so-and-so! hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ jaḥ svāhā

Having filled the mouth completely with nectar,
One should please all the deities.
One should meditate on the yogis and yoginīs,
And all actions will be accomplished.
oṃ bhakṣa sarba yogiśvarī hūṃ hūṃ jaḥ svāhā

Just as the actions and the rest are fully applied,
So one should fully apply the torma rites and the rest.
Pacifying, enriching, subjugating,
Summoning, killing, sowing division,
And paralyzing: through the very essence of the torma,
All the actions of the yogis
Should be accomplished. With the hook of wisdom
One should satisfy one's chosen deity.

In a field, a sacred site, or likewise a village,
In a town, or upon a mountain,
One should gather many bhūtas together;
Their host then assembles.
Through the conduct of all the channels
Their life force too is gathered in.
Gathered in the place of one's own knots,
Then the navel and the heart are intended.
By bowing with reverence to the yoginīs,
The practitioner will attain the supreme.
Dwelling always in the pīṭhas and upapīṭhas,
The deity will grant the supreme.
One endowed with experience in that place
Should practice by the rite described before.

To be rid of ulcers, old age,
Scabies, leprosy, and the like,
One should take the day of the bhūtas
And so practice, well settled in equipoise.
Having bathed with the water of the gods,
And especially with the five nectars,
One should anoint the limbs with great black oil
And offer incense of that same substance.
Within a square and a triangle
One must, with diligence, do this at the center.
Just as before,
One should have all the buddhas listen.
One should laugh eight kinds of laughter,
hā hā hī hī hū hū he hai:
So it is done by the practitioner.

Mounted properly upon the seed syllable of fire,
Opened up by wind,
Joined at the crown of the head,
Having made offering at the base of the earth,
One should set it in the center of the womb
And with diligence watch the melting.
In the center of the womb, in due order,
The yogi should imagine himself.
The light rays of the messengers gather on every side like clouds;
Behold an ocean of blazing garlands.
By contemplating in this way,
The abode of bliss of all yogas,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the rite of Kaṅkāla, the third chapter.

Next I shall explain the abiding in union
Of the samādhi of Bared Fangs.
The stage of drawing the bow comes about
Through the vajra yogi.
Imagine the drop of life brought forth;
Passing it through all the junctures,
Imagine the bow staff as its sign.
By urging on all the sounds,
The arrow, as it is drawn to the full,
Meeting at the navel of the central channel,
Should shatter the target of consciousness.
This is the slaying of all the sources.
In the great bliss as it travels,
There, through awareness, one goes elsewhere.
Through the applications of Bared Fangs
Renunciation comes about.
Destroying the inexhaustible target,
One will enter into people.
From the sphere of nirvāṇa
One enters into the towns of others.
The habitual tendencies of former emanations
Are continually destroyed there.
The great self of melting
Is bestowed in a peaceful form.
ṣṭi travels down to the soles of the feet
Like the thread of a jewel.
If at that juncture, in the ultimate sense,
One looks upon the three realms,
Then for all people
The target is distinguished by its characteristics.
As for all those senses,
What need to mention piercing them oneself?
Entering fully into other beings,
One will go most wondrously.
Abiding perfectly in the net of illusion,
Look upon all births.
The consciousnesses of the eye and so on,
Make them move inward!
Know that as the form of the ḍāka.

The chief in the form of the ḍākinī:
In emanating the maṇḍala first,
One should meditate on Bared Fangs and the rest.
One's own element is the form of bliss;
Each is made the chief in its own place.
The great ḍāka born from that,
The forms together with the ḍākinīs,
All in the heart of the various sources of phenomena,
Indestructible and peaceful,
All phenomena gathered into one:
The mantra practitioner should draw these according to the rite.
Having made the vowels and consonants equal,
Interchanged by their groups,
Which accomplish all desired aims,
The hero dwelling there should properly extract them.
The four bodies, the wisdom deity and the rest,
So called, will be attained without doubt.
Above and below, they always grant accomplishment;
One goes everywhere oneself as a messenger.

Fifty letters in eight groups:
That itself is the supreme and wondrous ḍāka.
Or else the maṇḍala of the vow
Is supreme, like the lesser tantra.
The measure of all the letters
Is to be indicated in the vows and so on.
Letters and names
Are expressed as the vows.
Maṇḍalas and tantras,
And likewise chapters,
All the collections of rites:
Doubled, short, and long.
Therein all are definitely victorious;
The one who emits and gathers in
Is here explained as the family of beings.
Such is the characteristic of each one;
The meditation on each one
Is to be understood in due order.
The name and form of a letter
Are held to be the form of beings.
As that secret mantra is recited,
One should place it thoroughly in that very thing.

The letter ā without the drop
Is red, free of the mother letters.
The letter a with the drop
Is therefore of the vermilion family.
The letter ī is held to be adorned
Like the guñjā flower.
The letter ī is the color of sindūra;
Know it as the excellent fifth.
The letter ū has the light of the rainbow.
The letter ū, the sixth,
Is like a pair of lightning flashes.
The letter ṛ is the color of smoke,
Subtle, its body cast off.
The letter ṝ has the form of darkness.
ḷ is like a crow's wing.
The letter ḹ is the color of vermilion.
The letter i is pale red.
ai is red in color like sindūra.
The letter o is like the sun,
A thousand light rays blazing upward.
The letter au is ever blazing,
With light equal to ten million suns.
The half moon, and likewise the drop:
The letter aṃ, the one explained before.
Two moons and strong dots:
The letter aḥ, likewise the king.
Thus there are sixteen emptinesses,
The cause of the purification of actions.
Intoxication, possession, delusion,
And mindlessness are the first emptiness.

Delusion, agitation, and subjugation
Swiftly grant everything.
Thoughtlessness and intoxication
And possession by grahas are dispelled.
By meditating on the action of emptiness,
One comes to stupefy and to subjugate.
Utter delusion and bewilderment come about;
The body is moistened and bereft of thought.
Bringing under control and summoning,
Supreme is the one possessed of wisdom and the knowable.
The actions of expelling and of melting:
They are held to be sevenfold.
As if holding down the earth with overwhelming might,
The unpraised and the deluded mind:
Never in this life
Will it come back again.
There is hatred, and reviling
By the whole world;
At odds with all the world,
It forsakes abiding even for an instant.
All the actions of the mind:
Therefore the three worlds are filled.
Having drawn bliss in through the mouth,
Breathless, the trickling of the line sinks down.
It is done with the branches of the mother letters.
Through the abode of the Lady of the Samaya Vow,
The nature of the great seal (mahāmudrā)
Is empty of duality, supremely free of defilement.
The buddha line, above and below,
Abides as a lotus fiber.
The vowels and consonants, condensed,
Arise from the body of the elements of the elixir.
The deities, Vajrasattva and the rest,
Are wholly transformed into faces.
Beneath it, and wherever else,
All the letters surely abide.
"Until separate appearances arise,
Perform the protection," so I have taught.
Mind becomes the corresponding vowel;
Mind has the nature of emptiness.
The perfect union of the two moons
Is the supreme, unchanging mudrā.
Thus too, according to the nature of the elements,
One should perform whatever action one wishes.
Again, the emptinesses that perform all things
Should each be set in its place.
The first two are placed on the two feet;
The thighs, the secret place, the waist,
The navel, the heart, and above,
The throat, and likewise the palate,
The mouth, the nose, and the ears,
The forehead, the head, and the back.
Through perceiving the name of one's own element,
One performs subjugation and the other actions.
Again, for each one in turn,
The actions are twenty-five in nature.
One who knows that very emptiness
Should constantly meditate within.

From the letter kā to the letter ma,
Listen, great goddess!
By these, all the gods of the three realms,
The asuras and the humans,
Surely for the practitioner's good,
All come under his control.
The twenty-five principles
Are perceived as ka and the rest:
Self, maṇḍala, and body,
Deity, guru, and mantra,
Consciousness, wisdom, and essence,
Time, cause, and likewise result,
Breath, elixir, and action,
Torma, ground, and the other mudrā,
Sense faculty, central channel, illusion,
Junctures, and likewise lineage,
Scripture and inference,
Each like a magical display, in due order.
Thus what is beautiful in these principles
Should be known through great wisdom.
The action expressed by its nature
Has the color of the fire at the end of time.
Just as the actions of subjugation and the rest
Are made clear in sets of five,
Red, black, and likewise white,
Yellow, and likewise green:
The aims the practitioner wishes to accomplish
Come about through the transformation of these very things.
The nine letters beginning with ya,
By the rite that applies,
Are declared to be the families of the aggregates and elements
And the nine limbs.
In due order, the intelligent
Should know them in sets of nine.
These actions are to be known
In the Samāja, the Saṃvara, the Uttara,
The Saṃvara, the Vajraḍāka,
The Udaya, the Tattvasaṃgraha,
The Vajrabhairava, and the Saṃpuṭa,
And elsewhere too, by the rites that apply.
Thus, extracted from the Abhidhāna of three hundred thousand,
Is the Laghusaṃvara;
From the letter a to the letter kṣaḥ,
Its chapters, in due order,
Are joined so as to possess reality.

Saṃvara, born of the Ḍāka Ocean,
Fully taught within the Khasama,
I shall now explain its means of accomplishment.
On the central lotus
One should vividly meditate on the form of a buddha.
Body Ocean, Avarice Ocean,
Desire Ocean, and Envy Ocean:
One should meditate on these in the four directions.
On the lotus petals, the yoginīs,
Vajra Ocean, Jewel Ocean,
Lotus Ocean, and Variegated Ocean,
Should be meditated on counterclockwise.
On the four petals are skull cups,
The form of the four oceans,
In which all things are ripened into nectar.
Again, in the four corners,
One should set out in due order
Yoga, Wisdom, Suchness,
And Liberation Ocean, counterclockwise.
Form, sound, smell, and taste,
Touch, phenomena, mentation, and mind:
All are to be known as oceans,
And are to be placed on the left and the right.
Earth and sky, tongue,
And ears: all are oceans,
And the oceans of wisdom and the knowable
Are to be placed in the former order.
The great goddesses who guard the doors
Are to be placed counterclockwise:
Elephant Ocean, Horse Ocean,
Lion Ocean, and Buffalo Ocean.
The goddesses who dwell in the corners
Are Illusion Ocean, Dream Ocean,
Secret Appearance Ocean, and Appearance Ocean.
Their colors are as in the other wheels.
This should be known as the maṇḍala
Of the great-souled Lesser Awakening.
Following the order of the eight charnel grounds,
One should meditate in the sequence of the sādhana.

From the mouth of the buddha form
A lotus stalk surely emerges,
White in color, greatly blazing,
A lotus of a thousand petals.
In its center one should meditate
On Ḍākārṇava Cakrasaṃvara.
One should visualize him with a thousand arms;
His faces are thirty-seven;
He dances on eighteen legs.
He has three eyes, of various colors,
A form with a crown of matted locks,
With a crossed vajra and a half moon.
At his back he holds Maheśvara (Śiva).
For the rest, the heroes and heroines
